RFC 0033 (Draft → Active) — envelope-completion contract (truncation vs schema-violation retry routing) (2026-05-20)

Spec text + error-code work for stage 3-continued (final) of the envelope LLM-contract-hardening track (RFCs 0030–0033). Closes spec gap E5 from ai-envelope.md §"Open spec gaps" (refusal-mode interaction with retry policies — worked example). Layers retry-routing semantics on top of RFC 0032's event vocabulary; introduces NO new event types. Capability advertisement (envelopes.reliability.completion) landed in the RFC 0032 schema batch since 0033 depends on 0032's event vocabulary structurally. Promotion under the bootstrap-phase steward waiver per the RFC 0021–0032 precedent. Remaining acceptance criteria (2 conformance scenarios, reference-host emission, spec gap E5 closure annotation) define the path to Accepted.

  • spec/v1/ai-envelope.md — extended with §"Envelope-completion criteria" between §"Production flow" and §"Replay determinism". Normates: (1) completion criteria — an envelope is complete only when both clean stop AND payload-validates hold; (2) truncation retry path — MAY retry with INCREASED output budget (RECOMMENDED 2× multiplier per truncationBudgetMultiplier); MUST NOT include corrective schema fragment; counts against limits.schemaRounds; exhaustion → envelope_truncation_unrecoverable; (3) schema-violation retry path — MAY retry with host-authored corrective system fragment (not from model output); MUST NOT increase output budget; counts against limits.schemaRounds; exhaustion → envelope_payload_invalid; (4) refusal path — MUST NOT retry (circumvention concern); fails with envelope_refused_by_provider; (5) recovery path — internal to parsing, no retry consumed. Priority rule when truncation + schema-violation both fail: treat as truncation (output budget is upstream cause).
  • spec/v1/observability.md — §"Envelope-completion retry routing (RFC 0033)" landed in the RFC 0032 batch as cross-track observability documentation. Summarizes the routing distinction; cross-references the two new error codes.
  • spec/v1/rest-endpoints.md — §"Common error codes" gains two new codes: envelope_truncation_unrecoverable (truncation retry budget exhausted while failure mode remained truncation; paired with envelope.retry.exhausted { finalReason: "truncation" } + cap.breached; distinct from envelope_payload_invalid which covers the schema-violation path) and envelope_refused_by_provider (provider returned explicit refusal; MUST NOT retry; error message MUST NOT echo refusal text per SECURITY invariant envelope-refusal-no-prompt-leak).
  • schemas/capabilities.schema.json — envelopes.reliability.completion sub-block landed in the RFC 0032 batch. Required distinguishesTruncation: boolean; optional truncationBudgetMultiplier: 1..8 (default 2; informational).
  • schemas/run-event.schema.json + schemas/run-event-payloads.schema.json — REUSES the six RFC 0032 envelope-reliability event types unchanged. This RFC introduces NO new event types; the retry-routing semantics layer on top of RFC 0032's existing vocabulary.
  • RFCS/0033-envelope-completion-contract.md — status Draft → Active; status-history block records the promotion + the remaining Active → Accepted path. Notes spec gap E5 closure pending follow-up doc-prose pass.

Compatibility: additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1 + §4. Two new error codes (no existing code changed); two new MUSTs (no existing MUST relaxed) — both MUSTs introduced in previously-undefined behavior space (the retry-routing distinction was silent in v1.1) and gated on capabilities.envelopes.reliability.completion.distinguishesTruncation: true. Hosts that don't advertise retain their legacy retry-routing behavior. Existing envelope_payload_invalid code unchanged (still the schema-violation-retry-exhaustion code).

RFC 0032 (Draft → Active) — envelope-reliability run-event vocabulary + line-448 scope clarification (2026-05-20)

Spec text + wire-shape work for stage 3 of the envelope LLM-contract-hardening track (RFCs 0030–0033). Carries the central design decision of the track: the ai-envelope.md §"Run event log integration" line-448 MUST NOT is scope-clarified (not relaxed) — "envelope-specific events" is read as per-envelope-kind routing events (which would create a parallel routing surface and remain FORBIDDEN), NOT as cross-kind operational events (retry, refusal, truncation, capability substitution, prompt composition, provider usage). The cross-kind operational allowance was always implicit in existing precedent (RFCs 0026 / 0027 / 0029 / 0031 shipped under the same interpretation); RFC 0032 §A makes the precedent explicit so future RFCs that extend RunEventType for operational concerns don't get blocked. Promotion under the bootstrap-phase steward waiver per the RFC 0021–0031 precedent. Remaining acceptance criteria (6 conformance scenarios, 2 SECURITY invariants envelope-refusal-no-prompt-leak + envelope-recovery-no-content-leak, INTEROP-MATRIX rows, reference-host emission) define the path to Accepted.

  • spec/v1/ai-envelope.md — line-448 prose CLARIFIED per §A. Original FINAL-v1.1 text quoted verbatim; clarified text replaces "envelope-specific events" wording with "per-envelope-kind routing events" + explicitly permits "cross-kind operational events" via RFC. Lists consuming RFCs (0026, 0027, 0029, 0031, 0032) so the precedent is permanent. Classified additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §4 (scope clarification of previously-undefined behavior; per-kind events remain forbidden after the clarification — NOT a MUST relaxation). New §"Envelope-reliability events" between §"Run event log integration" and §"Wire serialization": documents the six events + tier table + capability-handshake example + reason enum extensibility (closed enum + x-host-* pattern matching RFC 0031 §B precedent) + trust-boundary + replay determinism + OTel projection cross-reference.
  • spec/v1/observability.md — new §"Envelope-reliability events (RFC 0032)" + §"Envelope-completion retry routing (RFC 0033)" between §"Provider usage events" and §"Open spec gaps". Documents the OTel attribute mapping per event; envelope.refusal.refusalText exclusion from OTel by default (private side channel); the two SECURITY invariants with their gate timing; the RFC 0033 truncation-vs-schema-violation retry-routing distinction for cross-reference.
  • schemas/run-event.schema.json — RunEventType enum gains six new entries (envelope.retry.attempted, envelope.retry.exhausted, envelope.refusal, envelope.truncated, envelope.nlToFormat.engaged, envelope.recovery.applied) clustered after model.capability.insufficient and before agent.toolCalled (cross-kind operational cluster).
  • schemas/run-event-payloads.schema.json — _typeIndex gets six new entries; six new $defs ship with full payload contracts. The reason/finalReason enums use anyOf [closed enum, x-host-* pattern] for closed-enum + extension support per RFC 0032 §B.1 (incorporating the MyndHyve type-mismatch distinction). envelopeRefusal.refusalText documents SR-1 + prompt-content double-redaction with null-on-replay tolerance. envelopeRecoveryApplied documents the no-content-leak invariant (only path + byteOffset; never pre-recovery substrings). Schema description variant count bumped 55 → 61.
  • schemas/capabilities.schema.json — envelopes.reliability sub-block lands inside the RFC-0030 envelopes container. Required supported: boolean; optional events: string[] (closed enum of the six event names — hosts list only what they actually emit); maxRetryAttempts: 1..16; completion: { distinguishesTruncation, truncationBudgetMultiplier? } (the RFC 0033 sub-block lands here since 0033 depends on 0032's event vocabulary).
  • api/asyncapi.yaml — DEFERRED. The original RFC §"Affects" listed api/asyncapi.yaml (adds 6 channels) but the Phase-1 batch defers this to match the RFC 0026 / RFC 0027 / RFC 0029 precedent (those Active RFCs ship RunEventType extensions without per-event AsyncAPI message definitions). The catch-all AnyRunEvent message handles new event types automatically. Named-message AsyncAPI entries are decorative for canonical lifecycle events; if a future RFC tightens AsyncAPI to require per-event messages for every enum entry, this RFC's events join the family in the same batch.
  • RFCS/0032-envelope-reliability-events.md — status Draft → Active; status-history block records the promotion + the remaining Active → Accepted path.

Compatibility: additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1 + §4. No existing required fields changed; no existing optional fields changed type; no existing event-type shapes touched; no existing endpoints touched. The §A line-448 prose change is classified as scope-clarification per COMPATIBILITY.md §4 — the scope of "envelope-specific events" was previously undefined; this RFC defines it without relaxing the per-kind prohibition. Hosts that don't advertise capabilities.envelopes.reliability.supported: true see zero behavioral change; consumers iterating events by type-string handle unknown types gracefully (existing forward-compat guarantee).

RFC 0031 (Draft → Active) — envelope variant discrimination + model-capability declarations (2026-05-20)

Spec text + wire-shape work for stage 2 of the envelope LLM-contract-hardening track (RFCs 0030–0033). Promotion under the bootstrap-phase steward waiver per the RFC 0021–0030 precedent. Remaining acceptance criteria (4 conformance scenarios, SECURITY invariant model-capability-substituted-no-credential-disclosure, INTEROP-MATRIX rows, reference-host emission) define the path to Accepted.

  • spec/v1/ai-envelope.md — extended with §"Variant payload discrimination (normative)" between §"Schema discipline" and §"Envelope Contract". Normative SHALL: variant envelope payload schemas use anyOf + single-string-enum discriminator per branch (each branch declares the discriminator property in required, satisfies the Tier-1 subset, and carries exactly one enum value). oneOf is FORBIDDEN — unsupported across all three Tier-1 vendor strict modes (Gemini silently drops, producing a looser-than-declared schema — a silent correctness bug). Field-presence-based discrimination is FORBIDDEN. Backward-compatibility paragraph preserves v1.x envelopes already using the pattern or oneOf/field-presence (forward-looking only; a future RFC MAY tighten).
  • spec/v1/host-capabilities.md — extended with §"Model-capability declarations" between §host.aiEnvelope and §host.promptLibrary. Documents the four-step dispatch flow (capability check → optional substitution → emit telemetry → dispatch or refuse), the spec-reserved capability registry (structured-output, discriminator-enum, long-context, reasoning, function-calling) + x-host-&lt;host&gt;-&lt;key&gt; extension prefix, the recursive-substitution prohibition (RFC 0031 §"Unresolved questions" #3), and the orthogonality with RFC 0029 prompt resolution (capability check first, then prompt resolution).
  • spec/v1/node-packs.md — extended with §"Model-capability declarations on NodeModules" between §"Per-node requiresSecrets[]" and §"Runtime formats". Author-side surface paralleling requiresSecrets[]; cross-references the dispatch contract in host-capabilities.md. Distinguishes requires[] (host capabilities, per capabilities.runtimeCapabilities[]) from requiredModelCapabilities[] (model capabilities, per capabilities.modelCapabilities.advertised[]).
  • schemas/node-pack-manifest.schema.json — NodeModule shape gains two optional fields. requiredModelCapabilities: string[] (pattern restricts to spec-reserved or x-host-* identifiers; uniqueItems; maxItems: 32). fallbackModel: { provider, model } with provider lowercase-ASCII-pattern (must be in aiProviders.supported[]) and model min-length-1.
  • schemas/capabilities.schema.json — new optional top-level modelCapabilities block (sibling to runtimeCapabilities) with supported: boolean (required), advertised: string[] (the active model's capability set, derivable by clients at install time to check NodeModule compatibility without fallback), substitutionSupported: boolean (when false, hosts MUST refuse on any unmet capability — they MUST NOT attempt fallback even when NodeModule.fallbackModel is declared).
  • schemas/run-event.schema.json — RunEventType enum gains "model.capability.substituted" and "model.capability.insufficient" after agent.promptResolved (cross-kind operational cluster matching the provider.usage / prompt.composed precedent). These are NOT envelope-specific events in the line-448 sense — they fire BEFORE envelope emission during dispatch — so no carve-out is needed (RFC 0032 §A formalizes the carve-out for the envelope-reliability events).
  • schemas/run-event-payloads.schema.json — _typeIndex gets two new entries; two new $defs (modelCapabilitySubstituted + modelCapabilityInsufficient) ship with full payload contracts. The substituted event documents the all-or-nothing "[REDACTED]" redaction option for fallbackProvider/fallbackModel (medium-severity SECURITY invariant — workspace policy MAY treat multi-vendor posture as confidential). The insufficient event documents the no-recursive-fallback constraint (fallbackAttempted: true when the declared fallback itself failed, no chaining to additional fallbacks).
  • RFCS/0031-envelope-variants-and-model-capabilities.md — status Draft → Active; status-history block records the promotion + the remaining Active → Accepted path.

Compatibility: additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. No existing required fields changed; no existing optional fields changed type; no existing event types touched; no existing endpoints touched; no existing MUSTs relaxed. The discriminated-union normative SHALL applies forward-looking only — existing variant schemas using oneOf or field-presence remain conformant for v1.x. Hosts that don't advertise capabilities.modelCapabilities.supported: true treat requiredModelCapabilities + fallbackModel as opaque metadata; dispatch proceeds without checks. Old SDK event-loop consumers iterating by event-type string handle unknown types gracefully (existing forward-compat guarantee).

RFC 0030 (Draft → Active) — envelope reasoning field + Tier-1 structured-output subset (2026-05-20)

Spec text + wire-shape work for the lowest-risk additive entry in the envelope LLM-contract-hardening track (RFCs 0030–0033). Promotion under the bootstrap-phase steward waiver per the RFC 0021–0029 precedent. Remaining acceptance criteria (conformance scenarios, SECURITY invariant, INTEROP-MATRIX rows, reference-host emission) define the path to Accepted.

  • spec/v1/ai-envelope.md — extended with §"Reasoning field (normative)" between §"Universal kinds (normative)" and §"Vendor-namespaced kinds". Normative SHALL: every envelope payload schema defined by this spec SHALL support an OPTIONAL reasoning field of type string. Hosts SHOULD prompt the model to populate it on multi-step-reasoning kinds; hosts MUST NOT reject envelopes where reasoning is absent; hosts SHALL NOT route on reasoning contents. Per-schema posture table for the four universal kinds (schema.response deliberately omits). Strict-mode optional-field emulation pattern documented for vendor-kind authors who want OpenAI-strict portability. Three-surface reasoning-relationship paragraph distinguishes envelope reasoning from prompt.composed.systemPrompt|userPrompt (RFC 0027 host-composed prompt) and agent.reasoning.delta (RFC 0024 model thinking-tokens) — complementary, not redundant.
  • spec/v1/structured-output-subset.md (NEW, informative) — Tier-1 cross-vendor JSON-Schema intersection table (OpenAI strict ∩ Anthropic strict ∩ Gemini responseSchema) with per-row "last verified: 2026-05" annotations. Documents additionalProperties: false REQUIRED, every property in required, anyOf SUPPORTED, oneOf UNSUPPORTED (Gemini silently drops — silent correctness bug), nesting depth ≤5, property count ≤100, etc. Carries the strict-mode optional-field emulation pattern + per-vendor primary-source citations. Maintenance posture: routine "last verified" date refresh is non-RFC; substantive row support-status changes require follow-up RFC.
  • schemas/envelopes/clarification.request.schema.json — reasoning: string added as OPTIONAL property (NOT in required, preserving v1.1 backward compat for emitters that pre-date RFC 0030).
  • schemas/envelopes/schema.request.schema.json — same.
  • schemas/envelopes/error.schema.json — same.
  • schemas/envelopes/schema.response.schema.json — INTENTIONALLY UNMODIFIED (side-channel ack; no reasoning needed per RFC §A).
  • schemas/capabilities.schema.json — new optional top-level envelopes block with reasoning.{supported, promptDirective: "mandatory" | "advisory" | "off"} and tierOneSubsetCompliance: "strict" | "warn" | "off". additionalProperties: true on the envelopes block reserved for future RFC 0032 (reliability) and RFC 0033 (reliability.completion) extensions. Independent of the RFC 0031 modelCapabilities block (sibling top-level key, no collision).
  • RFCS/0030-envelope-reasoning-and-tier-one-subset.md — status Draft → Active; status-history block records the promotion + the remaining Active → Accepted path.

Compatibility: additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. No existing required fields changed; no existing optional fields changed type; no existing event types touched; no existing endpoints touched; no existing MUSTs relaxed. The new reasoning field is OPTIONAL on every schema it appears on. Hosts that don't advertise the new capability blocks see zero behavioral change.

Code-review follow-ups to dispatch + cascade clusters (2026-05-20)

Six findings from the senior protocol review of commits 0c79082 + 6bcaf85:

  • HIGH #1 — node.dispatched event type landed in the normative corpus. schemas/run-event.schema.json's RunEventType enum gains "node.dispatched"; schemas/run-event-payloads.schema.json gains $defs.nodeDispatched (required childRunId + childWorkflowId; optional childStatus); api/asyncapi.yaml adds the NodeDispatched message + channel binding under runEventsUpdates. RFCS/0007-dispatch.md §D now names the event explicitly as part of the next-worker decision contract. Closes the corpus-consistency gap where dispatch-cross-worker-handoff.test.ts + dispatch-input-mapping.test.ts were asserting on an undeclared event. Compatibility: additive (new enum variant; new optional payload schema).
  • MEDIUM #2 — SR-1 redaction-walk made normative across nested + open-shape payloads. spec/v1/ai-envelope.md §"Redaction (SR-1 carry-forward)" adds an explicit MUST that the recursive scrub walks every string-leaf / object-property / array-element including open-shape children (additionalProperties: true regions); cannot short-circuit at known-shape boundaries. schemas/envelopes/clarification.request.schema.json drops the MUST from the context property's description (JSON Schema descriptions are non-enforcing prose) and reframes it as a behavioral guarantee inherited from the spec text.
  • MEDIUM #3 — sample host now advertises the cascade-cancel + external-event interrupt profiles. apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/discovery.ts adds interrupts.profiles: ['openwop-interrupt-cascade-cancel', 'openwop-interrupt-external-event'] so the /.well-known/openwop document honestly reflects the implementation. Quorum + auth-required profiles deliberately NOT claimed (partial implementations).
  • MEDIUM #4 — core.dispatch config keys validated at registration. routes/workflows.ts §checkMappingCapability now refuses workflows that set fanOutPolicy != 'sequential', workerDispatchModel != 'child-run', or askUserRouting != 'auto' with capability_not_provided + requiredCapability details, instead of silently treating them as the implemented defaults. Production hosts that DO support these surfaces SHOULD remove the refusal.
  • MEDIUM #5 — parentRunId on cascaded run.cancelled is now contracted. schemas/run-event-payloads.schema.json $defs.runCancelled gains optional parentRunId; spec/v1/interrupt-profiles.md §"openwop-interrupt-cascade-cancel" documents the reason: 'parent-cancelled' + parentRunId pairing as a MUST and the 410 interrupt_gone (preferred) / 409 interrupt_already_resolved (back-compat) resolve-after-cascade contract.
  • MEDIUM #6 — partial-failure posture documented on the cancel-cascade walker. routes/runs.ts cancel handler now carries an explicit comment on what happens when a mid-walk storage write fails (parent + already-processed children stay cancelled; subsequent re-cancel attempts short-circuit on the terminal-status guard); flags the transaction-or-finalizer upgrade path for production deployers.

Conformance: 1303 / 0 failing (no regressions).

Compatibility: additive across all six. No required-field changes, no existing-event-shape changes, no MUST relaxations. New enum variants, new optional payload fields, new advisory advertisements.

RFC 0027 / 0028 code-review follow-ups (2026-05-20)

Closes the issues flagged in this session's /code-review pass:

  • Capability gate split (spec/v1/prompts.md §"Capability advertisement" + schemas/capabilities.schema.json prompts.endpointsSupported). Previously capabilities.prompts.supported: true was double-loaded as both the Phase A node-execution gate AND the Phase B REST-surface gate. The reference workflow-engine sample advertised supported: true without implementing the REST endpoints, leaving a contract gap where spec-compliant clients would get a 404 (route missing) instead of the spec'd 501 (capability not provided). New endpointsSupported gate cleanly separates the two axes. Reference host now honestly advertises supported: true, endpointsSupported: false; api/openapi.yaml 501 descriptions updated to cite endpointsSupported.
  • promptCompose.ts resolveSecret null check — host now throws prompt_secret_unresolvable when resolveSecret() returns null (credentialRef not provisioned in BYOK store). Prior behavior silently dispatched the redaction marker even when the underlying secret didn't exist.
  • SDK contract — OpenwopClient.prompts.{list, get, render, create, update, delete} lands in sdk/typescript/src/client.ts with matching PromptTemplate/PromptRef/PromptKind/PromptVariable/ListPromptsRequest/ListPromptsResponse/GetPromptRequest/RenderPromptRequest/RenderPromptResponse types in sdk/typescript/src/types.ts (re-exported from src/index.ts). Mutating endpoints honor the standard MutationOptions.idempotencyKey pattern.
  • Conformance scenario citations — driver.describe('RFC 0027 §X', ...) rewritten to driver.describe('spec/v1/prompts.md §Section', ...) across all three new scenarios. RFCs document the change; the spec doc is the contract.
  • INTEROP-MATRIX.md — new §"Capability adoption — RFC 0027 + RFC 0028 prompt library" cataloguing adoption status of prompts.supported and prompts.endpointsSupported axes across tracked hosts.

Compatibility: additive (the new endpointsSupported field is optional; absent = false).

Sample — RFC 0022 dispatch cluster (2026-05-20)

Drains the four remaining dispatch-* conformance failures (dispatch-input-mapping, dispatch-output-mapping, dispatch-cross-worker-handoff, dispatchLoop) by wiring core.dispatch + core.orchestrator.supervisor end-to-end in the workflow-engine sample. Implementation-only — no normative changes:

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/executor/scheduler.ts — buildGraph now detects back-edges via DFS coloring (WHITE/GRAY/BLACK, Cormen-canonical) and drops them from the working graph before Kahn's topological sort. Back-edges are keyed by ${src}\x00${tgt} (not edgeId — fixture edges arrive as {id, sourceNodeId, targetNodeId} per the schema, so edgeId is often undefined and an edgeId-keyed Set would collapse). Lets the supervisor → dispatch → supervisor cycle in RFC 0022 fixtures parse without cycle_detected; the dispatch node drives the loop internally. Forward cycles unrelated to dispatch still trip the topological-sort leftover check.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/bootstrap/nodes.ts — adds two node modules:

- core.orchestrator.supervisor (RFC 0022 §A): conformance-mock form. Reads mockDispatchPlan from config (array of {kind: 'next-worker' | 'terminate', ...} decisions) and emits the full plan as outputs.decisions[]. Defaults to [next-worker (empty workers), terminate] when no plan is configured so conformance-dispatch-loop works without fixture-side decisions. - core.dispatch (RFC 0022 §A + RFC 0007 §D): consumes the upstream supervisor's decisions array and drives the per-decision loop internally. For each next-worker decision spawns the named child workflow via dispatchSubWorkflow with inputMapping / outputMapping (or perWorkerInputMappings / perWorkerOutputMappings per RFC 0022 §D for cross-worker handoff). Each spawn emits a node.dispatched event carrying childRunId + childWorkflowId; each consumed decision emits a runOrchestrator.decided event.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/runs.ts — projectRunSnapshot now surfaces inputs (when non-null) so the conformance suite can read child.inputs.&lt;key&gt; to assert the parent→child variable projection from RFC 0022 §A.

Net conformance: 4 → 0 failing. 1298 passed / 0 failed / 48 skipped / 3 todo — the dispatch cluster is the last cluster the conformance suite was waiting on.

Schema + sample — parent/child cancel-cascade interrupt profile (2026-05-20)

Drains the remaining cluster of openwop-interrupt-parent-child conformance failures by wiring the cascade end-to-end in the workflow-engine sample, plus one additive schema relaxation for the redaction-shape test:

  • schemas/envelopes/clarification.request.schema.json — adds OPTIONAL context open-shape object to questions[].items. Surfaces an LLM-provided per-question metadata bag; the engine treats it as opaque and MUST run the same SR-1 redaction scrub over it as the rest of the payload (ai-envelope.md §"Redaction (SR-1 carry-forward)"). Closes the redaction-recursion conformance test that asserted three canary substitutions across questions[0].question + questions[1].context.trace.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/executor/subWorkflowDispatcher.ts — when the child reaches a non-terminal waiting-* state, the dispatcher returns the open child-side interrupt instead of treating the suspended status as a terminal failure. Lets the parent's core.subWorkflow node surface the suspension instead of dying with subworkflow_child_failed.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/bootstrap/nodes.ts — core.subWorkflow returns NodeOutcome.suspended with the child's interrupt kind when the child suspended, so the parent run reaches the matching waiting-* status (per inferWaitingKind in executor.ts:659).
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/runs.ts — POST /v1/runs/{runId}/cancel cascades: walks runs by parentRunId and cancels them with reason parent-cancelled, resolves any open child interrupts with a cascade marker, and emits run.cancelled events. GET /v1/runs/{runId} snapshot now surfaces a childRuns[] projection so callers can discover spawned children.

New interrupt_gone code added to OpenwopErrorCode.

The sample-side changes are implementation-only — no wire-shape break for hosts that already implement the openwop-interrupt-parent-child profile differently. Code-review pass on the 33-commit gap-closure batch (2026-05-20)

Addresses 6 findings from the senior protocol review of commits aed7c60..2ce21df:

  • HIGH #1 — subWorkflow recursion bound + cycle detection. apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/executor/subWorkflowDispatcher.ts: new MAX_SUBWORKFLOW_DEPTH = 32 (overridable via OPENWOP_MAX_SUBWORKFLOW_DEPTH). Walks the parent chain before spawning the child; refuses with subworkflow_depth_exceeded when depth exceeded OR subworkflow_cycle_detected when the child workflowId already appears in the ancestor chain. Closes the DoS vector via hand-crafted self-referential fixture.
  • HIGH #2 — waiting-external enum reaches OpenAPI + schema. schemas/run-snapshot.schema.json gains "waiting-external" in the status enum with normative prose tying it to interrupt-profiles.md §openwop-interrupt-external-event. api/openapi.yaml's inline status enum on the run-create response extends to match. spec/v1/interrupt-profiles.md §openwop-interrupt-external-event documents the new status mapping with a back-compat clause: hosts predating the enum addition MAY surface waiting-input instead.
  • MEDIUM #4 — hashRequestBody deterministic key ordering. apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/runs.ts: sorts object keys recursively before serializing so two equivalent requests with different key order hash identically. Not RFC-8785 canonical (no number/NFC normalization) — flagged for production deployers needing stronger guarantees.
  • MEDIUM #5 — RunSnapshot.interrupt + parent-linkage on the canonical type. sdk/typescript/src/types.ts: RunSnapshot gains optional interrupt?: {kind, nodeId, interruptToken?, callbackUrl?, data?} and parentRunId? / parentNodeId?. Drops the as Record&lt;string, unknown&gt; cast in projectRunSnapshot; surface is now properly typed end-to-end.
  • MEDIUM #6 — credential-shape regex with lookarounds. apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/byok/ephemeralRunSecrets.ts: switched from \b boundaries to negative lookbehind/lookahead ((?&lt;![A-Za-z0-9_])...(?![A-Za-z0-9])). \b treats _ and - inconsistently for these token classes; lookarounds anchor to alphanumerics + underscore explicitly, so credentials embedded in snake_case or kebab-case contexts still get scrubbed.
  • LOW #7 — docs/PROTOCOL-STATUS.md. Regenerated (JSON Schemas 27→30, RFCs 29→32 across RFC 0025 + 0027–0029).

HIGH #3 (in-memory state without persistence) deferred as documentation-only follow-up; the four sites (variables runtime, quorum votes, idempotency body hashes, child parent-node IDs) all carry their own header-level comments today, but a uniform future-work pointer is worth a single dedicated PR rather than touch-everywhere prose churn.

RFC 0030 / 0031 / 0032 / 0033 (filed 2026-05-20, Draft) — envelope LLM-contract-hardening track (2026-05-20)

Four-RFC track addressing structural gaps in the inbound LLM-contract surface (spec/v1/ai-envelope.md, RFC 0021): strict-output reasoning collapse (Tam et al., arXiv 2408.02442), cross-vendor JSON-Schema fragmentation (OpenAI strict / Anthropic strict tool use / Gemini responseSchema intersection is host-undocumented), envelope-reliability telemetry vocabulary fragmentation (each host emits bespoke retry/refusal/truncation events), opaque envelope-emitting-node-to-model dispatch routing (NodeModule.requires covers host capabilities but not model capabilities), and the truncation-vs-schema-violation retry-routing distinction (silent in v1.1, surprising-behavior-prone in practice). Modeled on the OpenWOP Protocol Hardening Proposal (2026-05-20, MyndHyve canvas-orchestrator host team); sliced into four independently-shippable additive RFCs that preserve the proposal's PR-sequencing while giving the line-448 MUST-NOT carve-out (RFC 0032 §A) its own focused review.

  • RFCS/0030-envelope-reasoning-and-tier-one-subset.md (NEW, Draft) — Stage 1, lowest-risk additive. Two complementary additions: (a) OPTIONAL reasoning field of type string on every envelope payload schema where multi-step reasoning improves output quality, with a SHOULD on host prompt-injection and a MUST-NOT on routing on contents (per Tam et al. reasoning-collapse mitigation); (b) new informative sibling document spec/v1/structured-output-subset.md documenting the Tier-1 cross-vendor JSON-Schema intersection (last verified 2026-05). Adds capabilities.envelopes.reasoning.{supported, promptDirective} and capabilities.envelopes.tierOneSubsetCompliance flags. Three conformance scenarios. SECURITY: envelope-reasoning-secret-redaction invariant lands alongside reference-host implementation (RFC 0027 §G staging precedent).
  • RFCS/0031-envelope-variants-and-model-capabilities.md (NEW, Draft) — Stage 2, codifying de-facto practice. Two complementary additions: (a) normative SHALL — variant envelope payload schemas use anyOf + single-string-enum discriminator per branch (Tier-1-portable; oneOf is cross-vendor incompatible per RFC 0030 §B); (b) NodeModule shape gains optional requiredModelCapabilities: string[] + fallbackModel: { provider, model } for portable model-capability gating with host-driven substitution. Five initial capability identifiers: structured-output, discriminator-enum, long-context, reasoning, function-calling; host-private extensions prefix with x-host-&lt;host&gt;-. Two new RunEventType entries: model.capability.substituted (MUST when substitution fires) and model.capability.insufficient (MUST when no viable fallback; pairs with capability_not_provided). Adds capabilities.modelCapabilities.{supported, advertised[], substitutionSupported}. Four conformance scenarios. SECURITY: model-capability-substituted-no-credential-disclosure (medium-severity) allows opt-in redaction of fallback coordinates when workspace policy treats multi-vendor posture as private.
  • RFCS/0032-envelope-reliability-events.md (NEW, Draft) — Stage 3, reliability vocabulary. Six new RunEventType entries: envelope.retry.attempted (SHOULD), envelope.retry.exhausted (MUST), envelope.refusal (MUST), envelope.truncated (SHOULD), envelope.nlToFormat.engaged (MAY), envelope.recovery.applied (MAY). Formally clarifies the scope of spec/v1/ai-envelope.md:448 MUST-NOT ("Hosts MUST NOT extend the RunEventType enum to add envelope-specific events") — the prohibition was scoped to per-envelope-kind routing events (one event mirroring each envelope kind, which would create a parallel routing surface that causationId-based handler routing already obviates); it does NOT prohibit cross-kind operational events that fire across many envelope kinds for shared operational concerns (retry, refusal, truncation, recovery). Same posture as RFC 0026 (provider.usage) and RFC 0027 (prompt.composed); RFC 0032 §A makes the precedent explicit. Adds capabilities.envelopes.reliability.{supported, events[], maxRetryAttempts}. Six conformance scenarios. SECURITY: two new invariants — envelope-refusal-no-prompt-leak (MUST redact provider refusal text against BYOK + prompt-content pipelines) and envelope-recovery-no-content-leak (recovery event MUST NOT carry recovered envelope substrings).
  • RFCS/0033-envelope-completion-contract.md (NEW, Draft) — Stage 3 cont., truncation-vs-schema-violation retry-routing distinction. Normates the envelope-completion criteria (clean stop AND payload-validates) and the MUST-distinct retry paths: truncation → increased output budget (RECOMMENDED 2× multiplier), NO corrective fragment; schema-violation → corrective system fragment, NO budget increase. Reuses limits.schemaRounds as the truncation-retry cap to bound the DoS opening. Reuses RFC 0032's event vocabulary (no new event types). Two new error codes: envelope_truncation_unrecoverable, envelope_refused_by_provider. Closes ai-envelope.md §"Open spec gaps" E5 (refusal-mode interaction with retry policies). Adds capabilities.envelopes.reliability.completion.{distinguishesTruncation, truncationBudgetMultiplier}. Two conformance scenarios.

Compatibility: additive across all four RFCs. No required-field changes, no existing-event-shape changes, no MUST relaxations. The RFC 0032 §A line-448 prose change is classified as scope-clarification (per COMPATIBILITY.md §4 row "New normative requirement on a previously-undefined behavior") — per-kind events remain forbidden after the clarification; the cross-kind operational allowance was always implicit in existing precedent (RFCs 0026, 0027 shipped under the same interpretation). Hosts that don't advertise the new capability blocks see zero behavioral change. RFCs sequenced so each is independently shippable: 0030 (lowest risk; ship first), 0031 (depends on 0030's Tier-1 subset for §A pattern reasoning), 0032 (depends on 0030 for capability-block structure; ships the line-448 clarification), 0033 (depends on 0032's event vocabulary). Empirical motivation derived from production traffic + cross-vendor research; primary sources (Tam et al., JSONSchemaBench, OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini vendor docs, Instructor/Pydantic-AI/jsonrepair prior art) cited in each RFC. Bootstrap-phase waiver eligible per the RFC 0021–0029 precedent.

Workflow-engine sample — idempotency body-hash mismatch (2026-05-20)

Per idempotency.md §Layer 1: same Idempotency-Key + different request body MUST return 409. Suite delta: 1233 → 1234 passing / 14 → 13 failing.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/runs.ts — hashRequestBody(body) computes a SHA-256 over the JSON-serialized request body. In-memory idempotencyBodyHashes Map stores {key → bodyHash} at successful claim. On replay, compare hashes; mismatch returns 409 + idempotency_key_replay_mismatch + details.idempotencyKey. Body-hash storage is in-memory by design — a process restart loses the mismatch check (the cached response itself still replays from persistent storage, just without the bonus body-divergence detection).

Workflow-engine sample — quorum-aware approval gate (2026-05-20)

Implements interrupt-profiles.md §openwop-interrupt-quorum end-to-end. Suite delta: 1231 → 1233 passing / 16 → 14 failing.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/bootstrap/nodes.ts — core.approvalGate forwards requiredApprovals + rejectionPolicy + approversList from config into interrupt.data so the resolve handler sees the quorum policy.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/interrupts.ts — recordQuorumVote(...) maintains an in-memory per-interrupt vote ledger ({accepts: voter[], rejects: voter[]}). On each POST /v1/runs/:runId/interrupts/:nodeId:

- Single-approver gate (requiredApprovals ≤ 1): null return → fall through to normal resume. - Quorum gate, accept count < threshold: emits interrupt.vote.recorded event with ledger snapshot, returns 200 WITHOUT resuming. - Quorum met (accepts ≥ requiredApprovals): clear ledger, resume normally. - Majority-reject (rejects ≥ ⌊requiredApprovals/2⌋ + 1): clear ledger, mark interrupt resolved, fail run with error.code: &#39;approval_rejected&#39;. Ledger is in-memory by design — votes are ephemeral per suspend cycle.

Closes 2 conformance tests: interrupt-quorum-resolution &gt; three accepts resume to completed + > majority reject fails the gate. Compatibility: implementation-only.

Workflow-engine sample — streams.ts validation-before-content-negotiation (2026-05-20)

Fixed an ordering regression introduced by 0b8d010 (JSON content negotiation on /events). The negotiation branch was running BEFORE parseModes(...), so an invalid streamMode value combined with Accept: application/json returned 200 JSON instead of 400. Now mode + bufferMs validation runs first; the JSON branch only intercepts well-formed requests.

Closes 3 regressions: stream-modes > invalid streamMode is rejected, stream-modes-mixed > rejects streamMode=updates,bogus, stream-modes-mixed > rejects streamMode=values,updates. Suite delta: 1228 → 1231 passing / 19 → 16 failing.

RFC 0027 / 0028 / 0029 (filed 2026-05-19, Draft) — prompt-library track (2026-05-19)

Three-RFC track introducing portable, versioned prompt templates as a first-class wire-shape — closing the long-standing gap where core.ai.callPrompt config (workflow-chain-packs.md:62, host-capabilities.md:347) and AgentManifest.systemPrompt | systemPromptRef (RFC 0003) accept prompt bodies but offer no shared addressing, library distribution, variable schema, or observability. Modeled on the myndhyve reference impl's PromptEntry / PromptLibrary / WorkflowPromptService.resolveForExecution() shape; ported into protocol-level normative text via three independently-shippable additive RFCs.

  • RFCS/0027-prompt-templates.md (NEW, Draft) — Phase A wire shape. Introduces PromptTemplate (templateId + version + kind + text + typed variables + modelHints + tags), PromptRef (stringy prompt:templateId@version form + structured object form), shared prompt-kind.schema.json enum $def, capabilities.prompts advertisement block with observability: off | hashed | full, and prompt.composed RunEventType for cross-host multi-agent debuggability. SECURITY: composed-body emission gated by capability; secret-source variables MUST appear as [REDACTED:&lt;id&gt;] markers; untrusted-content trust-marker propagation per RFC 0021 §E + RFC 0020 §D. Two new SECURITY invariants (prompt-composed-secret-redaction, prompt-composed-trust-marker) land alongside reference-host implementation per the RFC 0021 staging precedent.
  • RFCS/0028-prompt-library-endpoints.md (NEW, Draft) — Phase B surface. Adds six REST operations under /v1/prompts* (listPromptTemplates, getPromptTemplate, renderPromptTemplate, createPromptTemplate, updatePromptTemplate, deletePromptTemplate), with :render carrying the deterministic-hash invariant that mirrors prompt.composed. New kind: "prompt" pack manifest (schemas/prompt-pack-manifest.schema.json) parallel to RFC 0013 workflow-chain packs; Ed25519 signing + SRI integrity reuse the existing pack pipeline. Capability extension adds packsSupported, mutableLibrary, library to the §0027 block. Five new conformance scenarios.
  • RFCS/0029-prompt-override-hierarchy.md (NEW, Draft) — Phase C semantics. Normates a four-layer resolution chain — WorkflowNode.config → AgentManifest → WorkflowDefinition.defaults → host built-in — for each (nodeId, kind) pair. Extends AgentManifest with optional promptLibraryRef + promptOverrides (RFC 0003 oneOf constraint preserved verbatim). Extends WorkflowDefinition with optional defaults.promptRefs. Adds agent.promptResolved RunEventType carrying the chain trace with applied: true on the winning layer so cross-host debuggers can render which ref applied and why. Three new conformance scenarios.

Compatibility: additive across all three RFCs. No required-field changes, no event-shape changes on existing events, no MUST relaxations. Hosts that don't advertise capabilities.prompts see zero behavioral change. RFCs sequenced so each is independently shippable: 0027 ships the shape, 0028 adds the surface, 0029 adds the semantics. Bootstrap-phase waiver eligible per the RFC 0021–0026 precedent.

All tokens are existing design-system variables; zero new CSS variables.

Workflow-engine conformance harness — wire required env vars (2026-05-19)

npm run test:conformance (the sample's in-process conformance bootstrap at apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/conformance/run.ts) now sets two env vars that the suite needs against this sample:

  • OPENWOP_RATELIMIT_DISABLED=true — the suite issues 1200+ requests in a short window; the 60-req/min per-IP rate limiter would otherwise 429-cascade and mask real failures.
  • OPENWOP_AUTH_DISABLE_COOKIES=true — the suite asserts 401 on missing credentials per auth.md §3. Default behavior auto-issues an anon session cookie which silently grants access and shifts the 401 to 200/201.
  • OPENWOP_TEST_SEAM_ENABLED=true — already needed for envelope/accept and the wrap/capability seams the suite drives.

Both flags are spec-aligned for a black-box conformance run; the comment on each call site warns production deploys NEVER to set them. Closes the auth: missing credential > returns 401 failure plus prevents the rate-limit cascade that was masking ~30 unrelated tests at the start of a full-suite run.

Workflow-engine sample — bulk-cancel endpoint + idempotency replay header (2026-05-19)

Two surgical wire-shape gaps. Suite delta: 1200 → 1206 passing / 47 → 41 failing.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/runs.ts — new POST /v1/runs:bulk-cancel per rest-endpoints.md §"POST /v1/runs:bulk-cancel". Returns 200 + {results: [{runId, ok, status?, error?}]} with per-id outcomes preserving request order. Validates runIds array (non-empty, ≤100 entries — details.maxRunIds on over-cap). Idempotent: re-bulk-cancelling already-terminal runs returns ok: true with the existing terminal status. Uses an Express regex path because : in a route string is path-to-regexp's parameter marker.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/runs.ts — openwop-Idempotent-Replay: true header now set on cache-served POST /v1/runs responses per rest-endpoints.md POST /v1/runs response-headers. Clients distinguish replayed responses from fresh ones (same runId + status — header is the only observable signal).

Two related conformance failures stay deferred: (1) bulk-cancel > mixed-outcome needs node-input variable resolution (delay node receives 0ms instead of the seeded 30s, so the run completes before cancel hits); (2) idempotency > same key + different body returns 409 needs request-body-hash on IdempotencyRecord for replay-mismatch detection. Both are their own session units.

Compatibility: implementation-only. New endpoint + new optional response header on existing endpoint. No wire-shape, schema, or other reference-host changes.

Workflow-engine sample — getWorkflow endpoint + strict streamMode validation (2026-05-19)

Two surgical wire-shape gaps closed against the workflow-engine sample. Suite delta: 1198 → 1200 passing / 49 → 47 failing.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/workflows.ts — new GET /v1/workflows/{workflowId} per api/openapi.yaml operationId=getWorkflow. Looks up via workflowCatalog.getWorkflow(id) so both runtime-registered workflows and auto-loaded conformance fixtures resolve. Returns 200 + the workflow definition; 404 workflow_not_found on unknown ids. registerWorkflowRoutes(...) now takes hostSuite for catalog access.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/streams.ts — parseModes(...) now reads streamMode (per spec) in addition to legacy mode; refuses unknown modes with 400 + unsupported_stream_mode instead of silently falling back to ['updates']. Adds the values-is-exclusive rule per stream-modes.md §Mixed mode — values MUST NOT be combined with other modes (the engine would emit the more permissive set, so the constraint adds nothing; refuse so clients catch the mistake early).
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/types.ts — OpenwopErrorCode gains unsupported_stream_mode per the canonical error envelope.

Closes 3 conformance failures: route-coverage > GET /v1/workflows/{workflowId}, stream-modes > invalid streamMode is rejected, stream-modes-mixed > rejects streamMode=values,updates with 400.

Compatibility: implementation-only. The legacy ?mode= query parameter still works; ?streamMode= is preferred per spec. No wire-shape, schema, or other reference-host changes.

Workflow-engine sample — artifact-route auth stub (2026-05-19)

Closes the artifact-auth conformance gap on the workflow-engine sample. Port of the SQLite host fix (3c2b5d6 earlier this session): GET /v1/runs/{runId}/artifacts/{artifactId} requires an explicit Bearer token; the auto-issued anon session cookie is not sufficient. Closes the info-leak surface for every HTTP method (401 → 405 → 404 stack, in that order). Suite delta: 1194 → 1198 passing / 52 → 49 failing.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/runs.ts — new app.use(...) middleware that pattern-matches /v1/runs/{runId}/artifacts/{artifactId}, returns 401 unauthenticated when no Authorization: Bearer … header is present, 405 method_not_allowed for non-GET methods, and 404 not_found for authed GETs (no artifact storage end-to-end yet — positive-path lights up when an artifact-producing fixture lands).

The sample's auth middleware auto-issues anon session cookies on missing-auth requests so they otherwise fall through to the catch-all 404. Conformance test asserts 401 specifically — meaning the artifact endpoint MUST gate on a real bearer principal, not an anon. Mirrors the SQLite host's posture.

Compatibility: implementation-only (workflow-engine sample). New route added; no contract or wire-shape changes elsewhere.

First slice of the variables-runtime build that the dispatch / subWorkflow tier rests on. Closes the identity-passthrough conformance scenario (fixtures.md §conformance-identity): inputs.{name} on POST /v1/runs lands in RunSnapshot.variables.{name} on GET /v1/runs/{runId}, deep-equal round-trip. Workflow-engine sample — POST /v1/runs returns 201 (spec-aligned) (2026-05-19)

The sample previously returned HTTP 202 Accepted on POST /v1/runs based on the "Cloud Tasks dispatch is async" rationale. Per api/openapi.yaml (line 188) and the conformance suite's runs-lifecycle.test.ts:42, the spec mandates 201 Created — the run resource exists immediately, execution is async but that's an implementation detail. Wire-shape gap unblocked by the prior fixture-loader commit (e2ed9a8).

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/runs.ts — both res.status(202) sites flipped to 201 (line 163 POST /v1/runs synchronous path, line 326 idempotency-replay path). Idempotency cache write at line 158 also updated to persist 201 so subsequent replays surface the spec-compliant status.
  • Sample internal tests — smoke.test.ts (3 sites), auth-cookies.test.ts (1), rate-limit.test.ts (3 — including the test-internal app.post('/v1/runs', ...) mock), storage-adapter-parity.test.ts + storage-adapter-parity-testcontainers.test.ts (1 each on responseStatus) flipped to assert 201. All 211 sample-internal tests pass.
  • Conformance — runs-lifecycle.test.ts now passes 3/3 against the sample (was 2 failing). Other downstream fixture-gated scenarios that pre-validate run creation are also unblocked.

Compatibility: implementation-only (workflow-engine sample). No spec, schema, OpenAPI, or other reference-host changes. The spec already documents 201; this brings the sample into line.

RFC 0026 follow-ups + missing-await bug in callAI invocation-log lookup (2026-05-19)

Resolves five code-review findings against the RFC 0026 Active → Accepted promotion commit, plus a pre-existing async-await bug that the new test caught.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/aiProviders/aiProvidersHost.ts — pre-existing bug fix: callAI consults getInvocationLog().get(cacheKey) (async since commit 1093ce3 "async Storage interface + Postgres adapter"), but the value was being cast as AiCallResult | null without await. The cast left a Promise object in cached, the truthy check fired, and every callAI returned early via the cache-hit branch with a spread of the Promise — yielding {credentialRefHashed} only. Two missing awaits added (get and put). Without this fix, core.openwop.ai's BYOK ctx.callAI(...) path returned empty results — observable as missing content / usage on the LLM result. Restores the documented per-call cache semantics.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/aiProviders/aiProvidersHost.ts — RFC 0026 §B fix: the provider.usage emit moved out of the callAI boundary and into the body of dispatchPlain. Reason: dispatchStructured's parse-retry loop calls dispatchPlain up to 3 times; at the callAI boundary it was emitting only 1 event for N upstream provider invocations, collapsing the per-attempt token records into the final-attempt's figures and violating §B "MUST emit exactly ONE per LLM provider invocation". Now: each dispatchPlain call emits its own event. callAIWithTools and callAIManaged retain their own per-function emits — those code paths have no internal retry. The AdapterScope.emit doc comment was reworded to describe the cache-hit early-return semantics precisely (the original phrasing conflated per-attempt cache with run-replay).
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/providers/usageEmitter.ts — refactor: buildProviderUsagePayload() now delegates to buildProviderUsagePayloadFromTokens() after running its extractors. Removes ~15 lines of duplicated payload assembly + rate-table cost computation. Behavior unchanged.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/test/provider-usage-emit.test.ts (NEW) — three vitest scenarios covering the real aiProvidersHost → dispatchPlain → emit path. Mocks globalThis.fetch with synthesized OpenAI-style SSE streams. Verifies (1) plain callAI emits exactly 1 event with correct token figures; (2) structured callAI with parse-fail-then-succeed produces 3 events with correct per-attempt token correlation; (3) the emitted payload contains neither the cleartext API key nor the prompt content (§D trust boundary). Adds black-box coverage of the wire-up that previously had only seam-level testing.
  • schemas/capabilities.schema.json — providerUsage block description dropped stale (Draft) label after the RFC 0026 promotion. Matches the convention used by RFC 0022 / 0023 / 0024 capability blocks.
  • RFCS/0026-provider-usage-event.md — §"Active → Accepted" status history updated to reflect the corrected emission site (per-invocation inside dispatchPlain, not per-callAI-boundary).

Compatibility: additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. No wire-shape, schema, OpenAPI / AsyncAPI, prose, or endpoint contract changes — only impl-side refinements + a pre-existing bug fix.

Workflow-engine sample — fixture-loader foundational fixes (2026-05-19)

Two foundational fixes that turn the workflow-engine sample into an actual conformance target. Before this commit, the sample loaded 62 conformance fixtures into the in-process Map but downstream consumers couldn't see or run them: discovery advertised them at the wrong path, and every loaded fixture threw cycle_detected on execution because the executor's nodeId field was unset.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/host/index.ts — fixture loader normalizes nodes[].id (the conformance fixture authoring shape per conformance/fixtures/*.json) to nodes[].nodeId (the executor's WorkflowDefinition shape per executor/types.ts:264). The scheduler's Kahn-algorithm topological sort consults nodeId exclusively (executor/scheduler.ts:101); without normalization, every fixture appeared as a graph of undefined-keyed nodes and the cycle detector threw cycle_detected on the first run.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/discovery.ts — moved fixtures: listLoadedConformanceFixtures() from inside the capabilities block to the top-level discovery doc. The conformance suite reads c.fixtures at the top level (per conformance/src/lib/fixtures.ts:80); the nested location meant setAdvertisedFixtures() collapsed to "no fixtures advertised" and every isFixtureAdvertised(...) gate skipped. Mirrors the SQLite reference host's discovery shape.

Local verification: discovery now advertises 62 fixtures at the top level. POST /v1/runs {workflowId: "conformance-noop"} reaches status: completed (previously: cycle_detected). Downstream fixture-gated scenarios now run far enough to expose real wire-shape gaps (e.g., the sample returns HTTP 202 where the conformance suite expects 201 on POST /v1/runs) — those are separate fixes; this commit unblocks them.

Compatibility: implementation-only (workflow-engine sample). No wire-shape, schema, or endpoint contract changes — the top-level fixtures array already matches the spec shape that other reference hosts advertise.

Downstream-LLM untrusted-content wrap (2026-05-19)

Closes the aiEnvelope.trustBoundaryPropagation downstream-LLM-reconsume it.todo via a new pure helper wrapForLLMPrompt(...) and matching test-seam endpoint. Per ai-envelope.md §"Trust boundary" line 380: downstream LLM nodes that re-consume a RunEventDoc whose contentTrust === 'untrusted' MUST wrap the content with &lt;UNTRUSTED source=&quot;...&quot; type=&quot;...&quot;&gt;...&lt;/UNTRUSTED&gt; markers before reaching the prompt. The convention follows SECURITY/threat-model-prompt-injection.md (prompt-injection-input-marker / prompt-injection-kb-marker / prompt-injection-artifact-marker / prompt-injection-mcp-marker).

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/host/promptInjectionGuard.ts (NEW) — pure helper. wrapForLLMPrompt({contentTrust, payload, eventType?, source?, attributes?}) → string. Untrusted input → wrapped with stable attribute ordering for deterministic conformance matching; trusted (or absent) input → stringified payload only. Attribute escape is minimal (&, ", &lt;) — wrap is for prompt context, not HTML rendering, so full HTML escaping would over-encode.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/testSeam.ts — new endpoint POST /v1/host/sample/test/llm-prompt-wrap that exposes the helper directly. Lets conformance assert the wrap contract via the seam instead of having to drive a full LLM-node execution. Env-gated like the other seams; attribute filter drops non-primitive values.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/aiEnvelope.trustBoundaryPropagation.test.ts — 4 new behavioral assertions: (1) untrusted RunEventDoc payload wrapped with &lt;UNTRUSTED source=&quot;run-event&quot; type=&quot;...&quot;&gt;...&lt;/UNTRUSTED&gt; (payload preserved verbatim — wrap doesn't strip content, the threat model relies on the LLM honoring the marker); (2) trusted payload passes through unwrapped (over-marking trains LLMs to ignore the marker); (3) absent contentTrust defaults to trusted (no auto-mark); (4) MCP-tool wrap carries tool="..." attribute per threat-model line 95. Suite-wide it.todo() count: 4 → 3.

Compatibility: additive. New file, new sample-namespaced seam endpoint, new test cases. No wire-shape, schema, or endpoint contract changes.

Envelope-contract capability stacking refusal (2026-05-19)

Closes the aiEnvelope.contractRefusal capability-stacking it.todo. Per ai-envelope.md §"Capability handshake integration" (line 305): when a node typeId requires host.aiEnvelope: supported and the host doesn't advertise it, the refusal MUST fire BEFORE the per-envelope contract gates (host-gate, node-gate, schema-floor). Refusal code is capability_required per capabilities.md §"Unsupported capability — refusal contract" — observable as a distinct outcome from envelope_contract_violation.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/host/capabilityOverlay.ts — DEFAULTS gains host.aiEnvelope.supported: true. The workflow-engine sample IS the aiEnvelope acceptor surface so the honest default is advertised; conformance tests toggle off via the capability-toggle seam to exercise the refusal path.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/testSeam.ts — POST /v1/host/sample/envelope/accept consults resolveCapabilityFlag('host.aiEnvelope.supported') as the FIRST refusal layer, before any AcceptOptions setup. Returns EnvelopeOutcome.invalid with reason: "capability_required" + structured details citing the spec section. When the flag IS advertised (default), falls through to the existing acceptor flow.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/aiEnvelope.contractRefusal.test.ts — 2 new behavioral assertions covering both directions of the gate: (1) host.aiEnvelope.supported = false → capability_required refusal regardless of host-gate / node-gate match (proves the capability gate stacks ABOVE the envelope-contract gate); (2) host.aiEnvelope.supported = true → envelope-contract gates run normally (proves the layer is gated on the flag, not unconditional). Each test cleans up via resetHostCapabilities() in afterEach. Suite-wide it.todo() count: 5 → 4.

Compatibility: additive. No wire-shape, schema, or endpoint contract changes — new capability-overlay default + new first-layer refusal path in the sample-namespaced test-seam endpoint.

Approval-gate trust-boundary refusal (2026-05-19)

Closes the aiEnvelope.trustBoundaryPropagation approval-gate it.todo via a new approvalGateContext: boolean field on AcceptOptions. When set, the acceptor evaluates the post-normalization contentTrust and refuses with untrusted_content_blocks_approval if the value is 'untrusted' — encoding the ai-envelope.md §"Trust boundary" requirement that approval gates MUST NOT advance on envelopes whose content originated from an untrusted source (MCP tool result, A2A inbound, etc.) per SECURITY/threat-model-prompt-injection.md.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/host/envelopeAcceptor.ts — new optional AcceptOptions.approvalGateContext field; new "Step 6b" between trust normalization and BYOK redaction. Per-call decision: same envelope is valid in non-approval contexts (observation, log) and refused at the approval gate. Acceptor stays pure; the caller marks the call as an approval-gate resolution.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/testSeam.ts — POST /v1/host/sample/envelope/accept plumbs approvalGateContext through to the acceptor. Lets conformance assert the refusal contract via the existing seam instead of having to drive a real interrupt + resume flow.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/aiEnvelope.trustBoundaryPropagation.test.ts — 4 new behavioral assertions: (1) untrusted envelope + approvalGateContext → invalid + untrusted_content_blocks_approval; (2) run-level runTrustBoundary:"untrusted" propagates to the gate; (3) trusted envelope advances; (4) approvalGateContext absent → untrusted envelope still accepts (per-call gate decision, not envelope-global). Suite-wide it.todo() count: 6 → 5.

Compatibility: additive. No wire-shape, schema, or endpoint contract changes — new optional AcceptOptions field and new optional request-body field on the sample-namespaced test-seam endpoint.

Persisted envelope-correlation dedup seam (2026-05-19)

Backs the cross-process replay determinism contract from ai-envelope.md §"Replay determinism" end-to-end. The in-process priorCorrelations map dies with the process; this surface ensures a recovered process re-emitting the same correlationId reads back the original outcome from storage instead of re-running the handler against a (possibly now-different) capability surface. Closes the test-coverage debt's last seam-gated aiEnvelope todo. Suite-wide it.todo() count: 7 → 6.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/storage/storage.ts — Storage interface gains getEnvelopeCorrelation + putEnvelopeCorrelation. Insert-or-replace semantics matching putInvocation/putIdempotency. JSON-blob outcome carries the already-redacted payload from envelopeAcceptor.ts §"Step 7" — SR-1 redaction-carry-forward holds across the persistence boundary. recordedAt MUST be an ISO-8601 UTC (Z) string per the JSDoc constraint.
  • Schema migrations — sqlite v5 + postgres v3 add an envelope_correlations table with composite PK (run_id, correlation_id). sqlite v6 + postgres v4 add a run-scoped index idx_envelope_correlations_run to support future cleanup-on-run-terminal queries. Postgres uses TIMESTAMPTZ + ISO-string adapter coercion to match the sqlite TEXT shape.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/testSeam.ts — POST /v1/host/sample/envelope/accept gains optional persistedDedup: { runId: string }. When supplied, the acceptor's priorCorrelations is seeded from storage BEFORE consulting the in-band priorCorrelations array; the persisted entry wins on collision (cross-process source of truth). After a successful accept that wasn't served from cache, the outcome is persisted for future re-emissions.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/aiEnvelope.correlationReplay.test.ts — two new behavioral assertions: (1) persisted outcome replays the cached envelopeId even when the second call carries a DIFFERENT inbound envelopeId (proves cache-hit served the outcome, not a tautological re-mint); (2) persisted store enforces envelope_correlation_conflict across calls with no in-band priorCorrelations map.

Compatibility: additive. No wire-shape, schema, or endpoint contract changes — new optional request field on a sample-namespaced test-seam endpoint that's OFF by default outside conformance runs.

Conformance soak-gate close-out — opt-out axes + SQLite artifact stub (2026-05-19)

Previously the catch-all 404 fired before any auth check, letting an unauthenticated caller probe whether a runId/artifactId pair existed — closes that info-leak surface across every HTTP method while unblocking the artifact-auth scenario. Positive-path coverage stays host-pending (no reference host implements getArtifact end-to-end yet).
  • .github/workflows/conformance-soak.yml — wires both opt-out envs into the host-conformance job with inline comments explaining each entry. Distinguishes "host doesn't claim this surface" (good) from "host claims but doesn't deliver" (bug).
  • examples/hosts/sqlite/conformance.md — snapshot refreshed to 2026-05-19 with the new test counts + strict-mode opt-out env documentation.

Compatibility: additive. Wire-tier unchanged. Two new operator-side env vars, one host-side route that strengthens the auth posture without changing any documented contract.

  • sdk/typescript/ — 6 typed payload interfaces + 6 type guards + 1 high-level subscribeToAgentReasoning(ctx, runId, callbacks) helper that fans out agent.reasoning.delta and agent.reasoned into typed callbacks. Capability AgentsCapability.reasoning.streaming?: boolean added per RFC 0024. 22 new unit tests (incl. 6 behavioral tests for subscribeToAgentReasoning — arrival-order dispatch, handler-exception isolation, stop() idempotency, cancellation-vs-error discrimination, streamMode: 'updates' default); 43/43 pass.
  • sdk/python/ — new openwop_client.events module with 6 TypedDict payload classes + 6 runtime predicates + 6 typed extractor functions. Stdlib-only. 19 new unittest cases (incl. bool-as-int sequence rejection since Python's bool subclasses int); all pass.
  • sdk/go/ — new events.go with 6 typed payload structs + 6 predicates + 6 UnmarshalAgent*(ev) (payload, error) helpers. Sentinel ErrNotMatchingEvent for type-discriminator mismatches. 9 new test functions (incl. boolean-sequence rejection + empty-string delta acceptance per cross-SDK contract); all pass. go vet + gofmt -l clean on the new files.

Cross-SDK design symmetry: each language uses its idiomatic event-handling pattern (TS is narrowing, Python predicate + extractor, Go Unmarshal returning typed payload + error). Forward-compat preserved across all three — RunEventDoc.type stays open string, unknown event types return false from every predicate rather than throwing. Predicate semantics aligned across SDKs: each classifies wire shape (discriminator + required-field presence with correct primitive types) without enforcing schema-level minLength. The Go predicate's earlier empty-string guard was removed so wire-valid events like { agentId: "asst-1", delta: "", sequence: 0 } classify identically in all three SDKs.

This commit + the pre-existing reference-host implementations (commit 08ac7bc) resolve the SDK-helper acceptance line. Active → Accepted flip still gated on either (a) external host advertisement evidence in INTEROP-MATRIX.md, or (b) @openwop/openwop-conformance republish.

RFC 0026 (filed 2026-05-19, original Draft entry) — provider.usage event

New optional event type provider.usage added to the RunEventType enum + matching payload schema in schemas/run-event-payloads.schema.json#/$defs/providerUsage. Pairs with the existing OTel openwop.cost.* attribute group (per observability.md §"Cost attribution attributes") — the OTel surface is observability-only; this RFC lands the durable event-log sibling so replay reads it back deterministically, webhook subscribers receive it live, and external billing reconciliation has a fine-grained audit trail. 7-day comment window; bootstrap-phase waiver eligible per the RFC 0021-0025 precedent.

RFC 0013 (Workflow-chain packs) — Status: Draft → Active → Accepted (2026-05-18)

Promoted in two steps within the same day: Draft → Active once the 7-day additive-RFC comment window closed with no blocking comments (commits c4ec715, 664e363, 87153dc, 0189d6d had already merged Phases 1, 2, and 4 of the RFC's plan); then Active → Accepted once the reference-host expansion (Phase 3) landed on the in-memory host. The wire-shape contract is now exercised end-to-end against a live deployment, not just the pure-library server-free scenarios.

  • Reference host implementation: examples/hosts/in-memory/src/workflow-chain-expansion.ts (~330 LOC, zero runtime deps). Composes the spec-authoritative expandChain() algorithm with the host-specific I/O the spec deliberately leaves to implementers — filesystem registry resolution (default OPENWOP_PACK_REGISTRY_DIR=examples/packs/) + optional Ed25519 signature verification per node-packs.md §Signing (chain packs reuse the node-pack signing recipe verbatim). The pure expansion algorithm is a verbatim copy of conformance/src/lib/workflow-chain-expansion.ts (zero-runtime-deps policy on the in-memory host precludes cross-package import); the new live-host scenario asserts the two implementations stay in sync.
  • HTTP surface: POST /v1/host/sample/workflow-chain:expand, vendor-prefixed per host-extensions.md §"Canonical prefixes". Request body { packName, version?, chainId, parameters, parentWorkflowId? }; response { expansionId, chainId, packName, packVersion, nodes, edges }. Error-code → HTTP-status mapping: pack_not_found / chain_not_found → 404; pack_kind_invalid / pack_manifest_invalid / pack_signature_invalid / chain_unresolvable_typeid / invalid_request → 422; pack_signature_unverifiable → 500.
  • Capability advertisement: in-memory host now emits capabilities.workflowChainPacks: { supported: true } on /.well-known/openwop when the configured registry dir is readable at boot.
  • Tests landed:

- examples/hosts/in-memory/test/workflow-chain-expansion.test.ts — 5 cases, pure-function (positive 1-node + 2-node with edges + capability propagation, pack-not-found, chain-not-found, pack_kind_invalid for a mocked node-kind pack). - conformance/src/scenarios/workflow-chain-host-expansion.test.ts — 6 cases, capability-gated live-host scenario (discovery advertisement / 1-node + 2-node positive / pack_not_found 404 / chain_not_found 404 / invalid_request 422). Runs under OPENWOP_REQUIRE_BEHAVIOR=true against the in-memory host's HTTP endpoint.

  • Out of scope: Phase 4 vendor pack publications (the 55 unpublished editor presets from CANVAS-PACKS-INVENTORY) remain a downstream tracker. Public-registry publication of workflow-chain packs is gated on the same external security audit that gates the new core.openwop.* packs (per SECURITY/external-audit-engagement.md §2.1).

Compatibility: implementation-only. No spec / schema / wire-shape diff. The new endpoint lives under the vendor /v1/host/sample/* prefix already documented in host-extensions.md.

RFC 0024 (Draft) — Streaming agent.reasoned deltas (2026-05-18)

Closes the SOTA-parity gap between agent.reasoned (RFC 0002) and the live-reasoning UX shipped by Claude.ai extended thinking and ChatGPT o1. The existing agent.reasoned event fires once per closed reasoning block with the full trace, which means users see a "Thinking…" placeholder for tens of seconds and then a wall of text appears all at once. RFC 0024 adds a sibling event type agent.reasoning.delta and a capability flag capabilities.agents.reasoning.streaming so hosts that support reasoning models (MiniMax-M2.7, DeepSeek-R1, Anthropic extended thinking, OpenAI o1) can stream the trace incrementally — one event per token chunk — while the block is still open. The closing agent.reasoned event continues to fire with the complete authoritative content; existing consumers that read only agent.reasoned keep working without change.

  • RFCS/0024-agent-reasoning-streaming.md (NEW, Draft) — Summary + Motivation + Proposal (with schema diffs) + RFC 2119 normative semantics for emitter / consumer / replay determinism + Compatibility (strictly additive) + Conformance scenarios + 4 alternatives considered (including the rejected schema-overload approach) + 4 unresolved questions + Implementation notes + Acceptance criteria.
  • schemas/run-event-payloads.schema.json — Adds agentReasoningDelta $def with required {agentId, delta, sequence} + optional verbosity. Maps agent.reasoning.delta event type to it in the eventPayloads discriminator. Variant count bumped 48 → 49.
  • schemas/run-event.schema.json — Adds "agent.reasoning.delta" to the RunEventType enum.
  • schemas/capabilities.schema.json — Adds optional capabilities.agents.reasoning.streaming: boolean (default false). Hosts that omit it advertise the existing non-streaming contract.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/bootstrap/nodes.ts — Reference impl: chat-responder's managed-provider branch emits incremental agent.reasoning.delta events from the ThinkBlockSplitter's reasoningDelta callback, with a per-block sequence counter that resets at each closed block; the closing agent.reasoned carries the authoritative full content.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/discovery.ts — Sample host advertises capabilities.agents.reasoning.streaming: true honestly.
  • apps/workflow-engine/frontend/react/src/chat/hooks/useChatSession.ts — Reference FE: subscribes to both agent.reasoned and agent.reasoning.delta; deltas append to the in-flight thoughts.content buffer; the closing event finalizes it.
  • Phase 4 implementation cross-cut (2026-05-18, commit 685f540): the new agent.reasoning.delta event is now emitted by all three BYOK dispatchers (dispatchOpenAI via ThinkBlockSplitter for OpenAI-compat endpoints carrying &lt;think&gt;...&lt;/think&gt; content; dispatchAnthropic via the native thinking_delta content-block stream for Claude 4 with the thinking request parameter; dispatchGoogle via Gemini 2.5's part.thought: true flag with thinkingConfig.includeThoughts: true), not just the managed-provider tile. Dispatcher contract gains optional reasoningVerbosity field so callers explicitly opt into provider-side thinking; default is 'off' so workflow-runtime ctx.callAI paths don't see a surprise output-token regression (chat-responder paths explicitly pass 'full').
  • Compatibility: strictly additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. No existing event-payload shape changes; new event type is the new surface; new capability flag defaults to false. Old consumers see no change. RFC 0024 opens with the 7-day additive comment window per RFCS/README.md.
