Conformance: tighten agent.toolReturned causationId pairing (2026-05-18)

agentReasoningEvents.test.ts now asserts the second half of the RFC 0002 §B pairing contract: agent.toolReturned.causationId MUST equal the paired agent.toolCalled.eventId. Previously the scenario only validated callId correlation, which masked impl deviations on the strict event-log identity chain that replay-determinism guarantees depend on (spec/v1/replay.md §"Determinism with non-deterministic agents").

The new assertion is gated on the matched agent.toolCalled.eventId actually surfacing in the host's /events projection — hosts that omit eventId from their projection skip-equivalent (and SHOULD add it; the RFC 0002 §B chain isn't enforceable without it). The Postgres reference host already threads the eventId through via makeEventId(runId, calledEv.seq) in its core.conformance.mock-agent + core.mcp.toolCall executors, so the strict assertion passes for it without changes.

  • conformance/src/scenarios/agentReasoningEvents.test.ts — extended the events-type signature with eventId + causationId fields; added a second expect inside the pairing loop that asserts ret.causationId === matched.eventId when the host surfaces eventId on the matched event. Comment in the source spells out both pairing requirements with normative citations.
  • examples/hosts/postgres/test/mock-agent.test.ts — mirrored the strict assertion in the host-internal smoke so impl regressions on the eventId threading get caught locally (i.e., before downstream conformance runs).

Compatibility: behavioral tightening of an existing scenario. Hosts that already honor RFC 0002 §B (causationId MUST equal the paired agent.toolCalled.eventId) continue to pass. Hosts using callId-only pairing without setting causationId were previously passing the scenario despite a normative-MUST deviation; they now fail and need to ship the executor extension that returns eventId synchronously from appendEvent. Sequenced as a follow-up to RFC 0023's Accepted flip (a025a85) so the strict assertion lands as a conformance-only change rather than getting bundled into the RFC text.

RFC 0022 + RFC 0023 Active → Accepted (2026-05-18)

Both RFCs land at Accepted after their reference-host implementations clear the bar in RFCs/README.md ("Accepted once the implementation lands and the conformance suite reflects it").

RFC 0022 (core.dispatch + core.subWorkflow runtime variable mapping): All four happy-path scenarios (HVMAP-1a / HVMAP-1b / HVMAP-1c / HVMAP-2) now live behavioral against the Postgres reference host. The supervisor-mock extension flagged in §"Unresolved questions" #6 (config.mockDispatchPlan: OrchestratorDecision[] on core.orchestrator.supervisor) is implemented + documented as a third conformance hook on the supervisor's row in spec/v1/node-packs.md, alongside the existing mockConfidence / mockPendingDecision keys. The remaining it.todo() blocks (unset-variable projection, capability-refusal, child-failed/cancelled outputMapping skip, per-worker override precedence, mid-run no-propagation) require conformance-harness extensions tracked separately and do not gate Accepted.

RFC 0023 (Conformance agent-event emitters): The earlier deferral on "next @openwop/openwop-conformance release" was over-conservative. Per RFCs/README.md, Accepted gates on the conformance suite source tree reflecting the impl — not on npm publication cadence. The source tree carries the per-event-type assertion fix in agentReasoningEvents.test.ts; downstream consumers pick it up on their next republish. Reference host smoke passes both affected fixtures end-to-end.

Surface changes in this commit:

  • RFCS/0022-dispatch-input-output-mapping.md — Status Active → Accepted. Acceptance-criteria checklist all-ticked; Updated field records the flip rationale + history. The closing prose paragraph replaces the "Promotion to Accepted is gated on..." note with an "All acceptance criteria met" close-out.
  • RFCS/0023-conformance-agent-event-emitters.md — Status Active → Accepted. Last acceptance box flipped; Updated field records the over-conservative-deferral correction.
  • spec/v1/node-packs.md §core.orchestrator.supervisor row — "Conformance hooks" subsection expanded to enumerate all three conformance-only config keys (mockConfidence, mockPendingDecision, mockDispatchPlan) as bullets, with normative usage notes per key. Bullet structure replaces the prior single-paragraph format so each key's contract is independently scannable.
  • examples/hosts/postgres/src/server.ts — SUPPORTED_NODE_TYPES extended with core.identity, core.orchestrator.supervisor, core.dispatch. The host's executor switch already implemented these typeIds; the discovery-filter omission was silently downgrading orchestrator + dispatch + mapping fixture advertisement on capabilities.fixtures. The omission was load-bearing on the HVMAP scenarios' skip-vs-pass decision — adding the typeIds is what graduates them from "skipped against this host" to "exercised against this host."
  • CHANGELOG.md — this entry.

Compatibility: strictly additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. No wire-shape changes, no schema changes, no scenario-shape breakage. Status flips are bookkeeping. The SUPPORTED_NODE_TYPES extension is a fix to an under-advertisement on the reference host, not a contract change for hosts elsewhere.

Sample-host: managed "Try it free" provider tile (2026-05-18)

Adds a 4th provider option in the sample workflow-engine's BYOK wizard: a managed (server-held-key) tile labeled "Try it free". Underlying provider runs on MiniMax via the operator's MINIMAX_API_KEY; the underlying provider/model id is intentionally not exposed to users or downstream surfaces (event log, FE outputs all see openwop-free). Sign-in (Firebase OIDC, user:* tenant) gates the dispatch; anonymous tenants are refused with sign_in_required. Per-tenant per-day token cap (OPENWOP_MANAGED_DAILY_TOKEN_CAP, default 50000 combined input+output) enforced via a new sqlite table; daily_limit_reached returned once the cap is hit. Strictly sample-host scope — no spec or schema corpus changes.

  • apps/workflow-engine/providers.json — new entry id: "openwop-free" with managed: true, signedInHint: "Sign in to use", single placeholder model id auto (real model id stays in BE config).
  • apps/workflow-engine/frontend/react/src/byok/lib/providers.ts — ProviderConfig gains optional managed + signedInHint; validator relaxes the apiKey* field requirements when managed: true.
  • apps/workflow-engine/frontend/react/src/byok/BYOKWizard.tsx — managed-tile click triggers signIn.google() for anonymous users (with a localStorage pending-activation marker that auto-resumes after redirect return) and short-circuits the model + key entry steps when authed; writes a sentinel credentialRef: "managed:openwop-free".
  • apps/workflow-engine/frontend/react/src/byok/ConfiguredProviderCard.tsx — managed variant renders "Server-managed · daily usage limit applies" instead of a key-ref + key-delete affordance.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/providers/dispatch.ts — ProviderId gains 'minimax'; new dispatchMiniMax() calls the OpenAI-compatible chat-completions endpoint at MINIMAX_API_BASE_URL (default https://api.minimax.io/v1).
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/providers/managedProvider.ts (NEW) — server-held-key dispatch with sign-in gating, daily token cap, idempotent env-keyed bootstrap (encrypts via existing BYOK master key, persists to byok_secrets under managed:&lt;id&gt;), and result rewriting to hide the underlying provider/model ids past the module boundary.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/storage/sqlite/schema.ts — schema bumped to v4: new managed_provider_usage (tenant_id, date, provider_id, input_tokens, output_tokens) table.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/storage/storage.ts — Storage interface gains incrementManagedUsage / getManagedUsage. Postgres impl no-ops (managed-provider gating is sample-host scope).
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/bootstrap/nodes.ts + src/aiProviders/aiProvidersHost.ts — both AI entry points (chat-responder node + ctx.callAI host adapter) recognize the managed: credentialRef prefix and route through dispatchManagedChat, bypassing BYOK resolution. aiProvidersHost maps ManagedProviderError codes onto the canonical aiProviders error taxonomy.
  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/test/managed-provider.test.ts (NEW) — 14 tests across credential-ref helpers, bootstrap (no-op / seed / idempotent / rotate), gating (anon → sign_in_required, no-key → managed_unavailable, unknown provider → managed_unknown, cap-reached → daily_limit_reached), happy path (user-facing ids returned, usage incremented, underlying minimax literal absent from result).
  • Operator setup: set MINIMAX_API_KEY=... (and optionally MINIMAX_MODEL, MINIMAX_API_BASE_URL, OPENWOP_MANAGED_DAILY_TOKEN_CAP) and restart the BE; bootstrap encrypts and persists on first boot, rotates if the env value changes, no-ops if unchanged.
  • Compatibility: sample-host-only. No wire-shape, schema, or spec changes. Production deployers running the workflow-engine without setting MINIMAX_API_KEY see the "Try it free" tile render with the sign-in hint but the BE refuses dispatch with managed_unavailable until the env var is supplied.

RFC 0023 Draft → Active + reference-host implementation (2026-05-18)

The Postgres reference host now implements core.conformance.mock-agent per RFC 0023 §B, advertises capabilities.conformance.mockAgent: true (RFC 0023 §B.2), and the host's existing in-process test harness exercises both affected fixtures end-to-end. RFC 0023 promotes from Draft to Active per the bootstrap-phase steward waiver in CONTRIBUTING.md; the Active → Accepted flip is deferred to the next @openwop/openwop-conformance release that ships the per-event-type assertion fix in agentReasoningEvents.test.ts.

  • examples/hosts/postgres/src/server.ts — new case 'core.conformance.mock-agent' in executeNode honors the RFC 0023 §B emission contract: mockReasoning → agent.reasoned (verbosity-gated per capabilities.md §agents.reasoning); mockToolCalls[] → ordered agent.toolCalled / agent.toolReturned pairs sharing a host-minted callId (with causationId linking the returned event back to the called event's eventId per RFC 0002 §B); mockHandoff → agent.handoff with fromAgentId = the node's pinned agent.agentId and toAgentId from config; mockDecision / mockConfidence → agent.decided followed, when confidence is below the resolved escalation threshold (default 0.7, per-run override via RunOptions.configurable.escalationThreshold), by node.suspended { reason: 'low-confidence', agentId, threshold, observed } + run transition to 'waiting-approval' per spec/v1/interrupt.md §kind: "low-confidence". §B.1 gate enforced inline: the typeId is refused with typeId_refused for workflow ids outside the conformance-* prefix. SUPPORTED_NODE_TYPES extended to include the new typeId so the host's discovery filter advertises both rewritten fixtures.
  • examples/hosts/postgres/src/agent-events.ts — new REFERENCE_CONFORMANCE_CAPABILITY constant ({ mockAgent: true }).
  • examples/hosts/postgres/test/mock-agent.test.ts (NEW) — host-internal smoke against PGlite. Three checks: (a) discovery advertises capabilities.conformance.mockAgent: true AND both fixtures via capabilities.fixtures; (b) conformance-agent-reasoning produces ≥1 event from the closed agent.* set with schema-canonical agent-id fields per event type (agentId on reasoned/toolCalled/toolReturned/decided, fromAgentId/toAgentId on handoff) AND agent.toolCalled / agent.toolReturned pair via shared callId; (c) conformance-agent-low-confidence drives the run to 'waiting-approval' with a node.suspended { reason: 'low-confidence' } event whose observed &lt; threshold.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/agentReasoningEvents.test.ts — per-event-type assertion fix. The prior blanket payload.agentId check was over-strict against the canonical agent.handoff schema (which uses fromAgentId/toAgentId); the scenario now branches on event type. Closes a latent inconsistency between the suite and run-event-payloads.schema.json §agentHandoff.
  • RFCS/0023-conformance-agent-event-emitters.md — Status Draft → Active. Acceptance criteria checklist updated: 7/8 boxes ticked, with the Active → Accepted flip deferred to the next conformance-suite release.

Compatibility: strictly additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. No wire-shape changes. New typeId is conformance-only and gated by workflow-id prefix; production deployers SHOULD drop the typeId from their registry. New capability flag (capabilities.conformance.mockAgent) defaults absent; consumers tolerate. The conformance scenario change is a strict relaxation — hosts that previously emitted payload.agentId on agent.handoff (in addition to the canonical fromAgentId/toAgentId) continue to pass.

RFC 0023 (Draft) — Conformance agent-event emitters (2026-05-18)

Closes the "undocumented hooks on core.identity" gap surfaced by a downstream host (MyndHyve workflow-engine) that lost its conformance-agent-reasoning and conformance-agent-low-confidence passes after adopting the published core.openwop.flow.noop pack. The fixtures were leaning on config.emitReasoningTrace / config.mockConfidence hooks layered onto core.identity (a passthrough primitive); the hooks appear in no spec doc but are load-bearing in the conformance scenarios. RFC 0023 fixes the surface in three additive moves: (1) an Authorized-Emitters table normating which typeIds MAY emit each agent.* event from RFC 0002 §B; (2) a new conformance-only core.conformance.mock-agent typeId carrying the test hooks on a clearly test-scoped surface; (3) retroactive documentation of the existing mockConfidence / mockPendingDecision hooks on core.orchestrator.supervisor (already de-facto contract via conformance-orchestrator-low-confidence).

  • RFCS/0023-conformance-agent-event-emitters.md (NEW, Draft) — full proposal: §A Authorized-Emitters table (5 events × 4 typeId families); §B core.conformance.mock-agent typeId definition (5-key config: mockReasoning, mockToolCalls, mockHandoff, mockDecision, mockConfidence, plus optional agentId override); §C retroactive mockConfidence/mockPendingDecision documentation on core.orchestrator.supervisor; §D schema diff for core-conformance-mock-agent-config.schema.json; §E fixture rewrites. 5 alternatives (all rejected: noop hooks; orchestrator-direct rewrite; core.agent.emit-events in core; vendor.openwop.conformance pack; do-nothing). 4 open questions. Strictly additive — no wire-shape, event-payload, or RunEventType changes.
  • schemas/core-conformance-mock-agent-config.schema.json (NEW) — additionalProperties: false. Five config keys (RFC 0023 §B) with oneOf discipline on mockReasoning (boolean | object). mockDecision.confidence constrained to [0, 1]; same for mockConfidence shorthand. References error-envelope.schema.json for mockToolCalls[].error.
  • schemas/capabilities.schema.json — new capabilities.conformance object with mockAgent: boolean (RFC 0023 §B.2). Hosts that register core.conformance.mock-agent outside the conformance fixture prefix advertise this; conformance scenarios themselves remain gated on capabilities.agents.supported + capabilities.fixtures.
  • spec/v1/node-packs.md — new core.conformance.mock-agent row in the Reserved-typeIds table; updated core.orchestrator.supervisor row carrying the §C conformance hooks; new "Authorized emitters for agent. events" subsection (normative pointer to RFC 0023 §A) plus a normative core.conformance. namespace reservation.
  • conformance/fixtures/conformance-agent-reasoning.json — re-pinned from core.identity to core.conformance.mock-agent; config carries mockReasoning (object form with summary + tokenCount), a single-entry mockToolCalls, mockHandoff, and a high-confidence mockDecision so the §B emission contract drives the full agent.* family.
  • conformance/fixtures/conformance-agent-low-confidence.json — re-pinned to core.conformance.mock-agent; replaces the legacy mockConfidence: 0.5 shorthand with the explicit mockDecision: { decision, confidence: 0.5 } form. CP-1 contract unchanged (host MUST follow with node.suspended { reason: &#39;low-confidence&#39; }).
  • conformance/fixtures.md — catalog rows updated for the two re-pinned fixtures.
  • Compatibility: strictly additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. No wire-shape, event-payload, or RunEventType changes. The Authorized-Emitters table is normative going forward but does NOT invalidate any pre-RFC-0023 conformance pass — hosts that previously satisfied the affected fixtures via non-spec hooks on core.identity continue to pass via the rewritten fixtures, and hosts that bind agent.* emission to other typeIds (vendor extensions, LLM blocks) remain conformant. The capabilities.conformance.mockAgent flag defaults to false; hosts that omit the block continue to advertise spec-conformant capability surfaces.
  • Status flow: RFC opens Draft; promotes to Active after the 7-day additive RFC window. Reference-host implementation of core.conformance.mock-agent (registration in apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/executor/nodeRegistry.ts + new executor/nodes/conformanceMockAgent.ts) lands separately and is required for the Active → Accepted flip per the RFC's Acceptance criteria.

RFC 0022 — Postgres reference host implementation (2026-05-18)

Wires RFC 0022 §A (core.dispatch runtime variable mapping) + §B (core.subWorkflow inputMapping) end-to-end on the Postgres reference host. Closes 6 of the 7 RFC 0022 acceptance-criteria checkboxes (the 7th — Accepted promotion — is gated on supervisor-mock extension noted in §"Unresolved questions" #6, now added).

  • examples/hosts/postgres/src/agent-events.ts — REFERENCE_AGENTS_CAPABILITY gains dispatchMapping: true; new REFERENCE_SUBWORKFLOW_CAPABILITY exports { inputMapping: true }.
  • examples/hosts/postgres/src/server.ts core.dispatch block — rewritten as a sequential fan-out loop over nextWorkerIds[]. For each worker: read effectiveInputMapping = perWorkerInputMappings[workerId] ?? inputMapping ?? {}, project parent variables into the child run's inputs_json, await child terminal, then read effectiveOutputMapping = perWorkerOutputMappings[workerId] ?? outputMapping ?? {} and harvest child variables back into the parent's variables_json. Failed / cancelled children skip outputMapping per RFC 0022 §A. Distinct child runs per (parent, node, workerIdx) tuple so siblings get fresh idempotency surfaces. The shared parent variable bag is the cross-worker handoff channel per RFC 0022 §D.
  • examples/hosts/postgres/src/server.ts core.subWorkflow block — config.inputMapping is now read alongside outputMapping. Two-pass child variable seeding: variables[].defaultValue first, then inputMapping projections override matching keys (RFC 0022 §B normative bullet). Unset parent variables surface as undefined on the child variable.
  • /.well-known/openwop advertises both new capability flags.
  • conformance/fixtures/conformance-subworkflow-input-mapping{,-child}.json — parent + child fixture pair now drives the end-to-end test. Parent declares currentPrdId='prd-1', invokes the child via core.subWorkflow with inputMapping: { receivedPrdId: 'currentPrdId' }. Child declares receivedPrdId.defaultValue='baked-in' — RFC normates that inputMapping MUST override.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/subworkflow-input-mapping.test.ts — HVMAP-2 graduated from it.todo() to a live behavioral test. Inspects the child run's final variables via GET /v1/runs/{childRunId} and asserts receivedPrdId === 'prd-1'. Three sub-cases (unset parent var, no-midrun-propagation, refusal envelope) remain it.todo() with refined descriptions naming the fixture variants / harness hooks needed.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/dispatch-{input-mapping,output-mapping,cross-worker-handoff}.test.ts — top-of-file docstrings updated with the host's implementation status. The Postgres runtime wires the §A + §D surfaces; the dispatch-trio scenarios stay it.todo() pending the supervisor-mock extension (either a mockDispatchPlan config field on core.orchestrator.supervisor OR wiring the RFC 0023 core.conformance.mock-agent typeId to the supervisor side).
  • INTEROP-MATRIX.md Postgres row extended with a new "RFC 0022 reference impl" paragraph naming the capability advertisements + runtime behavior + the HVMAP-2 host smoke + the explicit it.todo() reason for HVMAP-1a/1b/1c.
  • **RFCS/0022-*.md** — acceptance-criteria checkboxes flipped (6/7 done; 7th — Accepted promotion — gated on supervisor-mock extension). New §"Unresolved questions" #6 documents the supervisor-mock decision.
  • conformance/fixtures.md — new row for conformance-subworkflow-input-mapping-child (the child fixture).
  • Compatibility: strictly additive. New capability flags default off; pre-RFC workflows that don't set mapping fields get bit-identical behavior. Sequential fan-out + outputMapping skip-on-failed-or-cancelled are normative per RFC 0022 §A.

RFC 0022 (Draft) — core.dispatch + core.subWorkflow runtime variable mapping (2026-05-18)

Closes the same authoring gap on the two openwop workflow-invocation primitives in one additive RFC. Both core.dispatch (RFC 0007 — has neither inputMapping nor outputMapping) and core.subWorkflow (node-packs.md §"core.subWorkflow contract" — has outputMapping only) lack a first-class way to project parent variables into child inputs at runtime. Without these, supervisor-driven workflows build hybrid DAGs that interleave dispatch + subWorkflow + side-channel storage — mechanical workaround that doesn't survive contact with non-trivial production cases. Found via the MyndHyve Launch Studio adaptive-supervisor rebuild (vendor.myndhyve.launchStudioSupervisor); RFC text ported from the MyndHyve internal draft at docs/rfcs/openwop-dispatch-input-output-mapping.md. Initially scoped to core.dispatch only; widened to cover core.subWorkflow after audit revealed the symmetric half-fixed gap.

  • RFCS/0022-dispatch-input-output-mapping.md (NEW, Draft) — full proposal: §A core.dispatch schema delta (4 new optional fields: inputMapping, outputMapping, perWorkerInputMappings, perWorkerOutputMappings); §B core.subWorkflow inputMapping field (symmetric to the existing outputMapping); §C capability advertisements (two flags, independently advertisable); §D fan-out interaction (sequential normative now, parallel deferred); §E positive + negative examples; 5 alternatives (all rejected); 5 open questions.
  • schemas/dispatch-config.schema.json — 4 new optional string→string map fields per §A.
  • schemas/capabilities.schema.json — agents.dispatchMapping: boolean + new top-level subWorkflow object with inputMapping: boolean.
  • spec/v1/node-packs.md §"core.subWorkflow contract" — new normative inputMapping field + two-pass seeding rule (defaultValue then inputMapping projection).
  • RFCS/0007-dispatch.md §References — adds forward link to RFC 0022.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/dispatch-input-mapping.test.ts + dispatch-output-mapping.test.ts + dispatch-cross-worker-handoff.test.ts + subworkflow-input-mapping.test.ts (NEW) — 4 it.todo() placeholder scenarios (13 cases total covering happy paths, override semantics, unset-variable projection, mid-run propagation suppression, and capability-refusal). Gated on the two new capability flags.
  • conformance/fixtures/conformance-dispatch-{input,output,cross-worker-handoff}-mapping.json + conformance-subworkflow-input-mapping.json (NEW) — 4 noop fixtures (the test scenarios drive the dispatch + subWorkflow surfaces directly; fixtures supply parent-variable defaults).
  • conformance/fixtures.md + coverage.md + README.md — catalog updates; scenario count 153 → 157.
  • Compatibility: strictly additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. All new fields are optional. Pre-RFC workflows get bit-identical behavior. New capability flags default to false. Workflows using the new fields fail-closed at registration on hosts without the matching flag (NOT silent acceptance).
  • Status flow: RFC opens Draft; promotes to Active after the 7-day additive RFC window OR the bootstrap-phase steward waiver per CONTRIBUTING.md. Implementation cycle (reference host + scenario promotion) lands separately under §"Acceptance criteria".

RFC 0021 §A acceptEnvelope reference implementation (2026-05-18, post-commit d84d3a8)

Closes the spec-to-impl loop for the RFC 0021 promotion. The workflow-engine reference host now actually runs the Ajv2020 gate that the spec section §A point 1-3 demanded; acceptEnvelope ships as a pure function the conformance suite exercises through an env-gated test seam.

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/host/envelopeAcceptor.ts (NEW, ~280 LOC) — implements the 6-step accept pipeline from spec/v1/ai-envelope.md §"Primitive": shape validation against ai-envelope.schema.json → kind validation against hostSupportedEnvelopes → per-kind payload validation against schemas/envelopes/&lt;kind&gt;.schema.json → Envelope Contract gate (nodeAllowedKinds) → engine-limit cap enforcement (clarificationRounds/schemaRounds/envelopesPerTurn) → trust-boundary normalization (returns normalizedMeta.contentTrust from envelope-supplied value OR runTrustBoundary propagation OR 'trusted' default). Defense-in-depth ISO 8601 check on meta.ts (Ajv2020 strict:false doesn't enforce format:"date-time"; the acceptor rejects malformed timestamps with a structured details entry).
  • apps/.../routes/testSeam.ts — adds POST /v1/host/sample/envelope/accept env-gated on OPENWOP_TEST_SEAM_ENABLED=true. Body: {envelope, hostSupportedEnvelopes?, nodeAllowedKinds?, runTrustBoundary?, counters?}. Response is the raw EnvelopeOutcome union (accepted / invalid / gated / breached). Mirrors the RFC 0020 test-seam pattern.
  • apps/.../routes/discovery.ts — FIXED pre-existing bug: supportedEnvelopes was advertising transport names (['rest', 'sse']) instead of AI envelope kinds (the field is the kind catalog per capabilities.schema.json). Now correctly advertises the 4 universal kinds from RFC 0021 §"Universal kinds (normative)" + populates schemaVersions[&lt;universal&gt;] = 1 for each. The transport list correctly lives in supportedTransports (already correct).
  • conformance/src/scenarios/ai-envelope-shape.test.ts — 8 BEHAVIORAL assertions through the seam: accepted / invalid (shape) / invalid (ISO 8601 timestamp) / gated (vendor kind not advertised) / breached (counter at cap) / universal-kind-always-allowed / normalizedMeta.contentTrust propagation from runTrustBoundary / envelope-supplied contentTrust precedence over runTrustBoundary. 18/18 tests pass (8 offline schema-compile + 8 behavioral + 2 HTTP-driven advertisement).
  • conformance/coverage.md — AI Envelope row grade upgraded C (shape-only) → B (shape + 8 behavioral accept-pipeline assertions); scenario list now includes ai-envelope-shape.test.ts.

Compatibility classification: additive + one sample-host safety-fix. The acceptor is purely additive — new file, new test seam, no openwop wire shape changes. The supportedEnvelopes correction in discovery.ts is a safety-fix at the sample-host layer per COMPATIBILITY.md §3: the field was always documented (since v1.0 in capabilities.schema.json) to carry AI envelope kinds; the implementation was advertising transports — wrong values. Consumers reading the field expecting envelope kinds were getting garbage; consumers reading it expecting transports were getting a value that has its own canonical home in supportedTransports. Both shapes were honest after the fix.

Storage adapter parity harness — SQLite vs Postgres via pg-mem (2026-05-18, post-commit d84d3a8)

Closes the "613 LOC unreviewed Postgres adapter" thread from commit 1093ce3. The new pg-based StorageAdapter now runs the same 19 Storage operations as the SQLite backend, validated via pg-mem (in-memory Postgres dev dependency) injected through vi.hoisted + vi.mock('pg', ...).

  • apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/test/storage-adapter-parity.test.ts (NEW, ~420 LOC) — 39 tests (19 unique × 2 backends + 1 integrity guard). Coverage: runs lifecycle (insert/get/update/list); events monotonic sequence (impl-defined offset); interrupts (insert/getByToken/resolve/listOpen); idempotency claim; webhooks; BYOK secrets (global + tenant-scoped isolation); audit append; tenant hard delete cascade; tenant reassign (anon → user migration).
  • Result: 39/39 pass. 15 sqlite tests pass behaviorally + 15 postgres tests pass behaviorally + 8 documented pg-mem-incompatible-skips (enumerated in PG_MEM_INCOMPAT) + 1 skip-set integrity guard that fails loud if a renamed test silently drops out of the skip set.
  • Documented pg-mem gaps (require real Postgres / testcontainers / live Cloud SQL to exercise; future drop-in): JSONB array param auto-stringification (webhook events); WITH ... INSERT ... RETURNING CTE atomicity (event sequence); INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING ordering (idempotency claim); cascading DELETE coverage (deleteAllTenantData).
  • Compatibility: test-only addition. No production code changes.

Closes the long-standing wire-shape gap where 8 v1 surfaces reference "AI Envelope" as a typed-wire concept without specifying the shape. Promotes the existing prose to FINAL v1.1, normates the 4 universal-kind payload schemas, and ships an in-tree conformance scenario asserting the contract.

  • spec/v1/ai-envelope.md — Status header DRAFT v1.x → FINAL v1.1 (date 2026-05-18). Prose body unchanged.
  • schemas/ai-envelope.schema.json — already shipped 2026-05-17 (parallel-agent draft). Description updated to reflect FINAL v1.1 status.
  • schemas/envelopes/{clarification.request,schema.request,schema.response,error}.schema.json — 4 universal-kind payload schemas (already shipped 2026-05-17). RFC 0021 §B normates each as $id under https://openwop.dev/spec/v1/envelopes/&lt;kind&gt;.schema.json with additionalProperties: false.
  • conformance/src/scenarios/ai-envelope-shape.test.ts (NEW) — 10 tests covering: capability advertisement shape (HTTP-driven, soft-skip without OPENWOP_BASE_URL); top-level + 4 universal-kind schema Ajv2020 compile (server-free); positive + 2 negative round-trip fixtures (server-free); advertised-universal kind ↔ schema linkage. 8/10 pass offline; 2 HTTP-driven skip cleanly without base URL.

spec/v1/ai-envelope.md — closes the AI Envelope specification gap (2026-05-17)

Eight v1 surfaces already reference "AI Envelopes" as a distinct wire concept (Capabilities.supportedEnvelopes + schemaVersions + the envelopesPerTurn/schemaRounds/clarificationRounds limits in capabilities.md + capabilities.schema.json; the ctx.aiEnvelope.generate({envelopeType, payload, ...}) contract in host-capabilities.md §host.aiEnvelope; the envelopeType: "prd.create" config on core.ai.callPrompt in workflow-chain-packs.md; the c.supportedEnvelopes.includes('clarification.request') derivation in profiles.md; vendor-namespaced kinds in host-extensions.md; "structured envelopes" in positioning.md; and envelopesPerTurn: 32 advertised by the reference host's routes/discovery.ts). None of these specified the envelope's own wire shape, its universal kinds, its per-kind schema discipline, its dedup/replay semantics, or its per-node permission set. This batch closes that gap as a DRAFT v1.x normative surface — additive, no breaking change.

  • spec/v1/ai-envelope.md (NEW, ~530 lines, DRAFT v1.x) — full normative prose. Defines the AIEnvelope wire shape (type / schemaVersion / envelopeId / correlationId / nodeId? / payload / meta / partial?), the four universal kinds (clarification.request / schema.request / schema.response / error) every engine MUST recognize, the vendor-namespacing rule that keeps core neutral (myndhyve-style prd.create / theme.create / tasks.create move under vendor.myndhyve.*), per-kind schema versioning via Capabilities.schemaVersions, the Envelope Contract gate (per-typeId accepts permission set with refusalMode: 'fail-node' | 'discard-and-warn'), the engine's accept-path ordering (shape → kind → payload → contract → limits → redaction → trust → dedup → handler), replay determinism via correlationId, trust-boundary propagation onto RunEventDoc.contentTrust, SR-1 redaction carry-forward from agent-memory.md, capability handshake integration (no new required fields; two optional ones envelopeStrictness + envelopeContracts.advertised), run event log integration (RunEventDoc.causationId = envelope.correlationId), wire serialization (direct JSON return from host.aiEnvelope.generate is normative; fenced-JSON extraction is informative), conformance scenario list, migration story for pre-v1.x hosts, and six open gaps (E1–E6).
  • Explicit distinction from RunEventDoc — the AI Envelope is the inbound LLM-emission envelope; RunEventDoc is the outbound event-log envelope, FINAL v1 and frozen. The two are complementary: LLM emits envelopes; engine emits RunEventDocs; the bridge is RunEventDoc.causationId = envelope.correlationId. The spec is explicit that this is not a duplication, an extension, or a replacement of RunEventDoc — it's the missing twin.
  • schemas/ai-envelope.schema.json (NEW) — top-level shape, JSON Schema 2020-12, additionalProperties: false. $defs for EnvelopeMeta and PartialInfo.
  • schemas/envelopes/{clarification.request,schema.request,schema.response,error}.schema.json (NEW) — per-kind payload schemas for the four universal kinds. Hosts publishing vendor kinds (vendor.&lt;org&gt;.&lt;kind&gt;) host their own schemas at the canonical {HostBase}/schemas/envelopes/{kind}.schema.json location per ai-envelope.md §"Schema discipline".
  • schemas/README.md — table extended with 5 new rows (one for the top-level envelope, four for the universals).
  • README.md — document index extended with the ai-envelope.md row right after agent-ref-positioning.md.
  • Compatibility: strictly additive per COMPATIBILITY.md §2.1. The Capabilities shape is unchanged at the required-field level (supportedEnvelopes + schemaVersions + the three limits were already required v1); two new optional fields (envelopeStrictness defaulting to "warn", envelopeContracts.advertised defaulting to false) are advertised behind absent → conformant. Pre-v1.x hosts that ship unnamespaced kinds (prd.create, theme.create) continue to validate; vendor-namespacing is RECOMMENDED at v1.x and REQUIRED at v2.
  • Not yet wired: reference hosts (in-memory / sqlite / Postgres / Python) do not yet implement the AI Envelope accept path — no INTEROP-MATRIX.md row claims envelope support today, consistent with the honesty principle. The conformance scenarios listed in ai-envelope.md §Conformance (aiEnvelope.{universalKinds,schemaDrift,correlationReplay,contractRefusal,trustBoundaryPropagation,redaction,capBreached}.test.ts) ship as part of this batch with it.todo() placeholders gated on OPENWOP_REQUIRE_BEHAVIOR=true + host advertisement of envelopeContracts.advertised: true; they graduate to behavioral assertions when a reference host wires the accept path.

apps/workflow-engine@P3 — Firebase Auth signup + Cloud SQL persistence + KMS-encrypted BYOK (2026-05-17)

Phase 3 of the app.openwop.dev reference deployment. Adds the signed-in tier on top of the existing anon cookie path so visitors can register, keep their work across sessions, manage BYOK credentials securely, and hard-delete the account when done. Implementation-only change — no spec / schema / wire shape diffs.

  • P3.1 OIDC bearer verification (apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/middleware/oidcVerifier.ts) — JWKS-based RS256 verification of Firebase ID tokens. Issuer-scoped tenant derivation (user:&lt;sha256(iss+&quot;:&quot;+sub).slice(0,32)&gt;) prevents cross-IdP sub collisions. Supports both RFC 7517 {keys:[...]} and Firebase's {&lt;kid&gt;:&lt;x509-pem&gt;} JWKS shapes. Pure node:crypto + global fetch, no npm deps. 6 hermetic test cases.
  • P3.2 Firebase Auth frontend (apps/workflow-engine/frontend/react/src/auth/) — Google + GitHub providers via Firebase JS SDK. useAuth() React hook, &lt;SignInButton&gt; header UI, onIdTokenChanged → setCurrentIdToken() so every fetch carries the fresh bearer.
  • P3.3 Cloud SQL Postgres backend (apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/storage/postgres/) — pg.Pool-backed Storage impl matching the sqlite + memory backends. Full async refactor of the Storage interface (every method Promise&lt;T&gt;). 9-table schema (runs, events, interrupts, webhooks, idempotency, invocation_log, workflows, audit_log, byok_secrets) with composite-PK tenant isolation. pg-mem hermetic tests for the schema round-trip. New DSN scheme: postgres:// / postgresql://.
  • P3.4 KMS-encrypted BYOK (apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/byok/kmsEncryption.ts) — Envelope encryption with per-record DEK. Google Cloud KMS for production; AES-256-GCM stub createLocalAesKmsClient() for hermetic tests. v2 record shape {wrappedDek, iv, ct, tag, kmsKeyName}. Resolver dispatches by tenant prefix: user: → KMS-encrypted persistent; anon: → ephemeral in-memory; everything else → legacy AES-256-GCM. 10 round-trip + tenant-isolation tests.
  • P3.5 Anon → user migration (apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/migrate.ts) — POST /v1/host/sample/migrate-tenant. Reassigns runs.tenant_id + workflows.tenant_id from the cookie's anon:&lt;sid&gt; to the bearer's user:&lt;sha&gt;, then re-encrypts ephemeral BYOK secrets under the user's KMS-wrapped DEK. Transactional in Postgres (BEGIN/COMMIT), txn in sqlite. Expires the anon cookie on success. SPA calls automatically after sign-in. 5 test cases.
  • P3.6 Tenant-scoped dashboard (apps/workflow-engine/frontend/react/src/runs/RunsIndexPage.tsx + GET /v1/runs) — Recent-runs list scoped to req.tenantId. Manual tenantId text input removed; tenant comes from the authenticated principal. Wildcard-bearer callers (conformance harness) can still pass ?tenantId= to filter explicitly.
  • P3.6.5 Account hard-delete (apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/src/routes/account.ts) — DELETE /v1/host/sample/account. Transactional cascade: events + interrupts by runId, then runs, workflows, byok secrets for the tenant. KMS-wrapped DEKs become orphan blobs (plaintext unrecoverable). SPA follows with firebase.auth().currentUser.delete() to revoke the IdP record. Audit log entry retained — security events outlive the account by design. Two-step confirm modal with explicit "Yes, delete everything" button.
  • P3.7 Deploy + docs — apps/workflow-engine/DEPLOY.md extended with Phase 3 steps (Cloud SQL instance, KMS key, Firebase Auth providers, Cloud Run env + Cloud SQL socket attach, frontend Firebase config). 100 / 100 backend tests passing; frontend tsc --noEmit clean.

apps/workflow-engine@P3 — production-rollout fixes (post-mortem) (2026-05-18)

Twelve issues caught while bringing P3 up on the live app.openwop.dev deploy. None changed the wire contract or the test counts; every one is documented inline in apps/workflow-engine/DEPLOY.md §"Phase 3 production-rollout gotchas (post-mortem)" so the next bootstrap doesn't repeat the debug cycle.

  • Cookie name → __session (commit cacf2d7). Firebase Hosting strips every cookie except __session when forwarding to Cloud Run. The original openwop.session was silently dropped on every API request, so the SPA minted a fresh anon tenant per request and the new GET /v1/runs always returned {runs:[]}. Renamed; configurable via OPENWOP_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME for non-Hosting deploys.
  • ajv declared as a prod dep (commit cacf2d7). src/host/mcpServerRouter.ts imports it but it was only present as a transitive of a dev-only package. The Cloud Run runtime stage's npm install --omit=dev dropped it → container failed to listen on PORT=8080.
  • DemoHostBanner Rules-of-Hooks bug (commit a582e25). if (user) return null; lived BEFORE a useEffect. When users signed in for the first time, the hook count dropped 4→3 and the whole SPA crashed on the sign-in commit with React error #300. Early return moved BELOW all hooks.
  • Modal portal (commit fd2090f). Sign-in + delete-account modals rendered BEHIND &lt;main&gt; even with z-index: 1000 because the &lt;header&gt; (their JSX parent) had position: sticky + backdrop-filter, both of which create stacking contexts. Switched to createPortal(modal, document.body) for both.
  • Modal centering (commit e74488d). align-items: center clipped the title above the viewport on narrow windows. Switched to align-items: start + padding-top: 8vh + overflow-y: auto.
  • Link contrast (commit a582e25). Bare &lt;a&gt; tags inherited the browser-default dark blue against the --color-bg: #0b0e14 dark surface, unreadable. Added a global anchor style using --color-accent.
  • Cross-provider account linking (commit 8335578). When a Google-registered user tries to sign in with GitHub (or vice versa), Firebase rejects with auth/account-exists-with-different-credential. New ExistingProviderSignInError captures the pending credential + existing provider list. After the user signs in with the existing provider, linkWithCredential attaches the rejected credential to the same Firebase user — subsequent visits work with either provider.
  • Redirect-based sign-in (commit 0bf42c3). Replaced signInWithPopup with signInWithRedirect to eliminate the persistent Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy would block window.closed console warnings that Firebase's popup poller triggers. State persisted across page loads via sessionStorage; processRedirectResult() runs once at boot and dispatches success / link-required / error.
  • Cache-Control: no-cache on index.html (commit b2a91c1). Firebase Hosting was caching index.html for 1 hour because a ** catch-all COOP rule was overriding the per-index.html rule (Hosting applies only the LAST-matching source). Collapsed into a single rule that matches non-asset paths and carries both headers.
  • authDomain → custom domain (commit e785890). signInWithRedirect returned a User but getRedirectResult returned null because the OAuth round-trip persisted state into the default-domain origin's storage; Safari ITP / Brave / Firefox TCP partition the third-party iframe's view of that storage on the SPA. Pointed VITE_FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN at app.openwop.dev (Firebase Hosting auto-proxies /__/auth/* on custom domains). Same-origin throughout → getRedirectResult returns the user as expected.
  • OAuth client redirect URI ops note (commit e785890). Firebase auto-syncs the default-domain redirect URI for Firebase-managed Google OAuth clients but NOT for custom domains. Manual step required in console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials. Same applies to GitHub OAuth apps (independent of Firebase). DEPLOY.md §13 now spells it out.
  • Local dev → prod proxy (commit cb77d5a). vite.config.ts defaults /api/** to forward to https://app.openwop.dev, so npm run dev in the frontend dir works against the live backend without spinning up a local Postgres / KMS / Firebase Auth. cookieDomainRewrite: '' strips the cookie's Domain attribute so the __session cookie binds to localhost. Override via OPENWOP_DEV_PROXY_TARGET=http://localhost:8080.

core.openwop.agents@1.0.1 — closes OPENWOP-AUDIT-2026-003 (raw-JS tool handler) (2026-05-17)

Patches the final remaining HIGH-severity finding from the steward-internal pre-audit (SECURITY/internal-pre-audit-findings.json). The 1.0.0 pack's agentRun() fallback invoked workflow-author-supplied tool.handler(call.arguments, ctx) as raw JS — breaking prompt-injection-tool-allowlist from SECURITY/threat-model-prompt-injection.md §"Authority bypass". The LLM (untrusted per §UNTRUSTED) decided which handler to invoke; the handler ran in the pack's process with the full ctx (including ctx.callAI, ctx.storage, secrets views).

  • packs/core.openwop.agents/index.mjs — rewritten agentRun() + new validateTools() boundary:

- Function-typed handler properties are refused with INVALID_TOOL_DECLARATION BEFORE any LLM call. - Tool declarations MUST be structured: { name: string, kind: string, ref?: string, argumentsSchema?: object }. Missing name or kind → INVALID_TOOL_DECLARATION. - Tool-driven runs (tools.length > 0) require ctx.agentRuntime.run (host-resolved registry per host-capabilities.md §host.agentRuntime). Without agentRuntime, refuses with HOST_CAPABILITY_MISSING + migration pointer. - Tool-less runs (tools.length === 0) still fall back to ctx.callAIWithTools with an empty tools array — no tool dispatch, no exposure. Safe path preserved. - model-selector regex hardened: malformed predicates are skipped (no uncaught SyntaxError), and prompts are length-capped at 4096 chars before evaluation (defense against catastrophic-backtracking ReDoS).

  • conformance/src/scenarios/agents-run-tool-allowlist.test.ts (NEW) — 7-case server-free scenario locking in the refusal semantics. Asserts function-handler refusal, name/kind validation, tools-without-runtime refusal, tool-less safe-fallback path, and agentRuntime thread-through.
  • SECURITY/invariants.yaml — new agents-run-no-raw-handler row (protocol-tier, severity high). Pairs with the existing reference-impl-tier prompt-injection-tool-allowlist for defense-in-depth (host validates tool-call envelopes; pack refuses raw-handler shape).
  • SECURITY/internal-pre-audit-findings.json — OPENWOP-AUDIT-2026-003.status: open → fixed. All 3 HIGH-severity findings from the pre-audit are now patched (#001 JWT alg-confusion in 2026-05-17 P0.1 batch, #002 SSRF in P0.1, #003 agent tool handler here). Honors the 7-day patch commitment recorded in SECURITY/internal-pre-audit-summary.md from 2026-05-17.
  • Compatibility: safety-fix per COMPATIBILITY.md §3. The 1.0.0 inline-handler fallback was a security defect; removing it is the patch. Workflows that supplied function-typed handlers in 1.0.0 now receive a clean error envelope with the migration pointer rather than silent code execution. Hosts that already implement ctx.agentRuntime see no behavior change.
  • Publication: built + signed + republished alongside this commit. verify-signatures.mjs confirms core.openwop.agents@1.0.1 against keyId=openwop-team-1.

core.openwop.ai@1.1.2 + core.openwop.mcp@1.1.1 — UNTRUSTED-marker discipline on ctx.trustBoundary='untrusted' runs (2026-05-17)

RFC 0020 §D follow-up. Closes the loop on trust-boundary propagation from the MCP server mount through the reference AI/MCP pack delegates.

  • core.openwop.ai@1.1.2 — adds applyUntrustedMarkers(messages, ctx.trustBoundary) helper at module top; wires it into the 7 ctx.callAI* call sites (chatCompletion, structuredOutput, embeddings, toolCalling, classify, extract, transform). When ctx.trustBoundary === 'untrusted' (today: every inbound MCP-server-mount tools/call run), user-role message content is wrapped in &lt;UNTRUSTED&gt;...&lt;/UNTRUSTED&gt; per SECURITY/threat-model-prompt-injection.md lines 68/118-123 BEFORE reaching the LLM. System-role content stays unmarked (workflow-author-controlled). Assistant-role content stays unmarked (downstream of the original wrap). Idempotent — re-feeding already-wrapped content does NOT double-wrap. Zero behavioral change for ctx.trustBoundary === 'trusted' or absent.
  • core.openwop.mcp@1.1.1 — invoke-tool now emits an optional outputs.markedContent string whenever (a) the host's MCP client flagged the response as untrusted OR (b) the run is operating under ctx.trustBoundary === 'untrusted'. The marker shape is &lt;UNTRUSTED tool=&quot;&lt;name&gt;">...&lt;/UNTRUSTED&gt; per the existing prompt-injection-mcp-marker invariant. Existing outputs.untrustedContent boolean preserved (back-compat). Schema $id bumps to /1.1.1/invoke-tool.output.json with the new optional property documented.
  • SECURITY/invariants.yaml — prompt-injection-input-marker and prompt-injection-mcp-marker invariants now cite apps/workflow-engine/backend/typescript/test/untrusted-marker.test.ts as the in-tree test path. Both stay tier: reference-impl (the assertion is on prompt strings, not wire shape); the in-tree test exercises the helper + delegate directly with mocked ctx. 14 tests, all passing.
  • Compatibility: safety-fix per COMPATIBILITY.md §3. The marker wrap is a defensive transformation that ONLY fires when the host explicitly flags a run as untrusted. Existing workflows (no trustBoundary set) see bit-identical behavior. The new markedContent field on invoke-tool is optional + additive.
  • Publication: in-tree only — built + signed (keyId openwop-team-1) + committed to registry/v1/packs/core.openwop.{ai,mcp}/-/1.1.{2,1}.*. Public publication to packs.openwop.dev remains gated on the external security audit per SECURITY/external-audit-engagement.md §2.1 / Track 7. registry/scripts/verify-signatures.mjs confirms 79/79 signatures over the registry (77 → 79 before/after this commit).
  • docs/PROTOCOL-STATUS.md regenerated.

core.openwop.ai@1.1.1 + core.openwop.crypto@1.0.3 — defensive parsing of model output + JWT shapes (2026-05-17)

Closes the MEDIUM JSON.parse audit findings from the post-publish review (third defect class after the http@1.1.2 idempotency-key bug and the 6-node pure-vs-non-deterministic batch). Three uncaught JSON.parse sites turned what should be clean error envelopes into TypeError / SyntaxError exits.

  • core.openwop.ai@1.1.1 — extract() (the structured-data extraction node) wrapped the LLM response parse in a fall-through expression: r.data ?? r.parsed ?? JSON.parse(r.text ?? r.content ?? 'null'). When the model ignored responseSchema and returned non-JSON text, the inner parse threw. Now mirrors the catch-and-fallback pattern already used by transform() in the same file: provider-parsed shapes (r.data, r.parsed) take precedence; non-JSON text surfaces as raw output with confidence: 0 so downstream nodes can branch on confidence rather than handle a Node runtime exception.
  • core.openwop.crypto@1.0.3 — jwtVerifyNode() hardened against malformed token shapes. Three new defensive returns (each with a clean { valid: false, reason: ... } envelope, additive to the existing reason set): malformed_token when the input isn't a 3-part dot-separated string, malformed_header when the header segment isn't valid JSON, malformed_claims when the claims segment isn't valid JSON (the latter is signature-verified-but-malformed — extraordinary but possible). Adjacent type guards on parsed-object shape (null/non-object) close the residual gap where a JSON null or 42 as the header bypasses property access. The 1.0.1 alg-confusion guard (JWT_VERIFIER_ALGORITHM_REQUIRED) is unchanged and still reached for valid-shape tokens.
  • Compatibility: safety-fix per COMPATIBILITY.md §3. The new malformed_* reason values are additive to the existing reason enum (signature / expired / not-yet-valid / iss / aud / etc.) — consumers checking specific known reasons fall through to their default case unchanged.
  • Publication: built + signed + republished alongside this commit (same flow as the earlier today's batch). 76 signed packs verified after publish.

core.openwop.data@1.2.1 + core.openwop.crypto@1.0.2 — correctness fix for nodes mis-declared as pure (2026-05-17)

Post-publish audit (see CHANGELOG [Unreleased] §"core.openwop.http@1.1.2" for the headline finding that motivated this sweep) surfaced 6 more nodes in the same defect class: declared role: "pure" + capabilities: ["cacheable"] while their runtime depends on wall-clock time or randomness. Under the engine's caching contract (node-pack-manifest.schema.json §"capabilities" — "engine uses these to decide caching"), a pure + cacheable node MAY be cached by input-hash, which for empty-input nodes like uuid collapses to "one cached value forever" and for time-dependent nodes returns stale data past expiration.

Patched (role: pure → side-effect, capabilities: ["cacheable"] → ["side-effectful"] — matches the canonical core.openwop.http.fetch declaration):

  • core.openwop.data@1.2.1 — data.uuid (uses randomUUID() — same defect class as the http.idempotency-key@1.1.0 bug; empty inputs → input-hash collision → same UUID forever under pure+cacheable) + data.datetime-now (uses new Date() — workflows expecting fresh wall-clock time would get a frozen value).
  • core.openwop.crypto@1.0.2 — crypto.jwt-mint (uses Date.now() for iat claim; cached mints never refresh iat/exp), crypto.jwt-verify (uses Date.now() for exp check; security: cached "valid" past token expiration), crypto.totp-generate (uses 30-sec window; security: defeats TOTP rotation per RFC 6238), crypto.totp-verify (uses 30-sec window; security: cached "valid" past window).
  • No runtime behavior changes — index.mjs files unchanged. The fix is purely the manifest's contract declaration: the engine now correctly caches outputs by (runId, nodeId) (replay-safe) and never by input-hash (no stale values across fresh executions).
  • Compatibility: safety-fix per COMPATIBILITY.md §3. The pre-fix declarations were incorrect — these nodes never satisfied the pure contract. Workflow authors who hit the input-hash cache path in 1.2.0 / 1.0.1 were getting worse behavior than the spec promised.
  • Yank policy consistent with the SSRF + JWT alg-confusion + idempotency-key batch: old versions remain immutable + accessible; deprecation flag flip lands ~2026-05-24 after the 7-day soak.
  • Publication: built + signed + republished alongside this commit (same flow as http@1.1.2). verify-signatures.mjs confirms both new versions against keyId=openwop-team-1.

core.openwop.http@1.1.2 — idempotency-key generator made deterministic (2026-05-17)

Closes a correctness/security bug in core.openwop.http.idempotency-key: the previous default mode (uuid) returned a fresh randomUUID() on every call, defeating the entire point of an Idempotency-Key. Workflow authors composing idempotency-key → fetch were getting a different key on every retry, so the remote service treated each attempt as a new request — exactly what idempotency keys are supposed to prevent. Caught during the post-publication review of the 17 core.openwop.* packs published 2026-05-17.

  • packs/core.openwop.http/index.mjs — idempotencyKey() rewritten. Default mode is now composite (SHA-256 of runId\0nodeId\0payload, key format openwop-&lt;sha256-prefix-16&gt; — matches the canonical formula already used by core.openwop.http.fetch's deriveIdempotencyKey). hash mode preserved for payload-only keys. The uuid mode is removed; calls with mode: uuid reject with CONFIG_INVALID and an error message naming the safety-fix.
  • composite now reads runId/nodeId from ctx (with inputs.runId / inputs.nodeId as overrides). Previously required them as inputs, which was awkward — the host already knows them. Both reference hosts (Postgres + SQLite) surface them on ctx.
  • Schemas bumped to /1.1.2/ — idempotency-key.{config,input,output}.json $id paths refresh; config enum drops uuid, keeps composite + hash; output gains a pattern: ^openwop-[0-9a-f]{16}$ constraint matching the new key format.
  • Compatibility classification: safety-fix per COMPATIBILITY.md §3. The pre-fix behavior was a correctness bug (the node's documented purpose is retry-safety; uuid mode could not deliver it). Workflows pinned to 1.1.1 and depending on the uuid mode silently get worse behavior on retry; pinning to 1.1.2 gets the correct behavior. Old versions (1.1.0, 1.1.1) will be flagged deprecated in the registry index alongside the existing crypto/http/rag deprecation batch (per [Unreleased] §"Pack patches: SSRF + JWT alg-confusion fixes (P0.1)" yank policy).
  • Publication: source fix only in this commit. Tarball build + signing + registry publication follow the same flow as the P0.1 batch — gated on the steward running scripts/build-pack-tarball.mjs + registry/scripts/verify-signatures.mjs.
  • No callers in-tree. grep across conformance/, examples/, apps/, packs/*/test/ finds zero references to core.openwop.http.idempotency-key. The fix's blast radius is whatever external workflows have adopted the node since 2026-05-13 publication of 1.1.0.
